| The hermetic-PATH coreutils symlink loop in `tests/test-terraform-pack-verify.sh:95-102` includes shell builtins (`printf`, `test`, `true`, `false`). Under `/bin/sh` these resolve via `command -v` to their bare builtin names (e.g. the literal string `"printf"`), so `ln -s "printf" "$coreutils_dir/printf"` creates a relative self-referential symlink that resolves to nothing. Four of the fifteen entries are broken symlinks on the current host. Tests pass anyway because `verify.sh` and the test stubs invoke these as builtins, not via PATH lookup. | Cosmetic noise only — no test depends on the broken symlinks resolving. Could mislead a future debugger who `ls -la`s the hermetic dir and assumes the fixture is corrupted. No behavior risk. | Cycle-2 self-review LR-1 flagged as LOW; tests pass; fixing is a five-minute follow-up not worth scoping into a fix-and-revalidate cycle. | Next time anyone touches that loop, OR if `ls -la` of the hermetic dir surfaces in a CI debug log and confuses a reader. Cleanest fix: drop `printf test true false` from the loop with a one-line comment that they are `/bin/sh` builtins; alternative is to guard `ln -s` on `case "$_resolved" in /*) ln -s ...;; esac`. | `docs/reports/self-review-2026-05-13-add-terraform-language-pack-cycle2.md` (GC'd by 30-day retention 2026-07-13; recover via git history) (Finding LR-1), `tests/test-terraform-pack-verify.sh:95-102` |
| The IaC-tool leak-guard in `tests/test-terraform-pack-verify.sh:106-110` uses `[ -e "$coreutils_dir/$_banned" ]`, which is false for broken symlinks under POSIX `test`. So if a future maintainer added `terraform` to the coreutils symlink loop on a host where `terraform` is absent, `command -v terraform` would return empty, the per-tool `[ -z "$_resolved" ]` check would fire first ("FAIL: required coreutil 'terraform' not found"), and the leak-guard would never even run. The intended threat model — a host where terraform actually leaks is a host where terraform is installed, so the symlink would be non-broken — holds, but the guard is one layer thinner than it could be. | Defense-in-depth gap, not a live bug. The "non-broken symlink leak" case is fully covered today; the "broken symlink leak" case is caught a step earlier by a different check with a less helpful error. | Cycle-2 self-review LR-2 flagged as LOW; current behavior fails fast and loud, just via the wrong error path for the typo case. | Next time the leak-guard is touched, OR when a maintainer hits the "required coreutil 'terraform' not found" error and is confused about why a banned tool name landed in the coreutils loop. Fix: change `[ -e ... ]` to `[ -L ... ] \|\| [ -e ... ]` (true for any symlink including broken, OR a real file) so the leak-guard's specific error message wins over the generic coreutil-missing one. | `docs/reports/self-review-2026-05-13-add-terraform-language-pack-cycle2.md` (GC'd by 30-day retention 2026-07-13; recover via git history) (Finding LR-2), `tests/test-terraform-pack-verify.sh:106-110` |
| Terraform ignore set in `.gitignore` / `templates/base/.gitignore` (block added by `03c5598`) diverges from GitHub's canonical `Terraform.gitignore` on four minor points: (a) `*.tfstate.*.backup` is narrower than canonical `*.tfstate.*` and misses workspace state (`terraform.tfstate.d/<workspace>/*.tfstate.backup`) plus `.terraform.tfstate.lock.info`; (b) only `*.auto.tfvars*` is ignored, not the broader `*.tfvars` / `*.tfvars.json` (defensible: explicit `-var-file=` fixtures often live in repo, but `prod.tfvars` with credentials is not protected); (c) `.terraformrc` and `terraform.rc` (Terraform Cloud token files) are not ignored — usually `$HOME`-scoped so risk is low; (d) the block-header comment "state and cache" understates scope (block also covers plan output, overrides, crash logs). | Low — current set covers the literal patterns the rule file (`terraform.md:11`) names and the most common state-leak vectors. Workspace state, project-local `.terraformrc`, and explicit-`-var-file` credential files are uncommon in scaffold-time setups. Comment wording is cosmetic. | Cycle-3 self-review surfaced all four as LOW maintainability findings (no CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM); broadening to canonical alignment would have required scope expansion (`!example.tfvars` negation design, additional template-mirror commits) during a fix-and-revalidate cycle with the pipeline cap already raised to 3. | ~~`leadActivityEventCount` (`internal/org/watch.go`, deadman's manifest-growth "lead activity" source) only excludes the watchdog's own cutoff-reason events; it is not scoped to `org_id` or to lead-attributable seats (`ev.SeatID == LeadIdentity`). Since `w.org.Manifest.Read()` reads the whole state-dir manifest, any unrelated seat's own event — or, in a shared state dir, a different org's event — still counts as "lead activity" and can prematurely clear a pending deadman alert.~~ (RESOLVED 2026-08-03 in feat/org-runtime-watchdog: cross-review AR-1 first added the `orgID` filter — skipping any event whose `OrgID` does not match. Self-review cycle-2 (finding M2-1) then added an `ev.SeatID == LeadIdentity` branch, but its own closure note ("fully closes this row") overclaimed correctness: self-review cycle-3 (finding H3-1) found that branch backwards — `ev.SeatID` on a `sent` event is the *recipient* (`Send` writes `SeatID: p.To`, `internal/org/verbs.go`), not the author, so `ev.SeatID == LeadIdentity` excluded the star topology's mandated seat→lead `sent` traffic while counting lead→seat sends as nothing. The cycle-3 fix corrects the model: every `sent` event is lead-authored BY CONSTRUCTION (only `ralph org send`, driven by lead/the operator, ever appends one — a seat's reply travels over the agmsg skill, which never touches this manifest), so `ev.Event == EventSent` now counts unconditionally, and the non-watchdog lifecycle allowance was widened from `stopped`/`disbanded` to the full lead-driven set (`spawned`, `spawn_started`, `stopped`, `disbanded`, `rejected`), closing a related cycle-3 gap (M3-1: lead spawning a *replacement* seat, not itself, in response to a stall ALERT). This row is now fully closed against the corrected model.) | A genuinely unresponsive Lead facing a real anomaly could have its deadman escalation silently cleared by unrelated seat/org manifest growth, weakening the human-escalation safety net AC-5 exists to guarantee. Not a regression from pre-PR behavior (deadman is new in this PR). | Self-review (M-4) recommended filtering to `ev.OrgID == status.OrgID && (ev.SeatID == LeadIdentity \|\| ev.SeatID == "")`, or dropping this source entirely in favor of the herdr/agmsg-derived sources (which are already lead-scoped). Cross-review AR-1 closed the `org_id` half; self-review cycle-2 (M2-1) closed the seat-attribution half with an inverted predicate; self-review cycle-3 (H3-1) corrected that predicate to the `sent`-is-lead-by-construction model and extended the lifecycle set (M3-1). | ~~PR⑤ (旧系撤去・ドキュメント全面改稿), or the first live multi-seat/multi-org deadman incident where a pending alert clears without genuine Lead activity — fix shape: scope `leadActivityEventCount` to `status.OrgID` and lead-attributable `SeatID`s.~~ Closed by cross-review AR-1 (org-scope), self-review cycle-2 M2-1 (seat-scope, later corrected) + cycle-3 H3-1/M3-1 (corrected model + spawn-side lead actions), all in feat/org-runtime-watchdog. | `docs/reports/self-review-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (M-4), `docs/reports/verify-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (AC-5 caveat), `docs/reports/test-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (Test gaps), `docs/reports/cross-review-triage-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (AR-1), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (Cycle 3, findings H3-1/M3-1), `internal/org/watch.go` (`leadActivityEventCount`, `checkDeadman`), `internal/org/watch_test.go` (`TestWatch_Deadman_SeatSentEvent_ClearsPendingAlert_WatchdogCutoffDoesNot`, `TestWatch_Deadman_LeadSpawnsReplacementSeat_ClearsPendingAlert`) |
<!-- RESOLVED 2026-08-03 in chore/org-debt-batch (Slice 4, commit ab4e9da): all four fixed. (1) `ResolveOrgStateDir`'s `source` is now surfaced in the `ralph org watch` startup banner and in `ralph status`'s state-dir line; `doctor` was left unchanged -- it has no org state-dir diagnostic line to annotate (its org-related checks (`checkOrgEnvelope`, herdr/agmsg availability, opt-in model probes) report config and tool availability, not state-dir resolution), so the doctor half of sub-item (1) was closed as not-applicable, not implemented. (2) `git status --porcelain` output is truncated to a bounded head (first 20 lines + "… N more") before building the scope-change ALERT body; the protocol-validation-failure fallback path now includes a `SEAT` header so Lead can identify the busy seat even when the full ALERT is rejected. (3) The unreachable `status.Escalated[alertID]` guard in `checkDeadman` was removed with a comment explaining why (`escalateAlert` already deletes the pending alert in the same statement); `Escalated` now prunes to the most recent 100 entries so `watch-status-<org_id>.json` no longer grows unbounded. (4) `evaluateTotalBudget`'s zero-active-seats guard now clears `Conditions[key].Active` when it fires, so a resumed org re-alerts on its next cutoff; existing regression tests were updated to the new semantics and a new resume→re-spawn→re-cutoff test asserts the re-alert. Row preserved for traceability. -->
| ~~4 deferred LOW findings from org-runtime-watchdog self-review, not fixed in the cycle each was first raised: (1) `ResolveOrgStateDir`'s `source` return is discarded (`_`) at both production call sites (`internal/cli/org.go:103,674`), so the resolved-precedence tier (flag/env/git-toplevel/cwd) is never surfaced in the `ralph org watch` startup banner or `status`/`doctor`, the one place it would earn its keep as a diagnostic; (2) an oversized scope-change ALERT — `git status --porcelain` output interpolated whole into the message body — silently degrades to a content-free protocol-validation-failure fallback (no `SEAT` header, no scope text) once it exceeds `DefaultMaxBodyChars`, so Lead sees only "message failed protocol validation" for a busy seat; (3) `checkDeadman`'s `status.Escalated[alertID]` guard is unreachable dead code (`escalateAlert` already deletes the pending alert in the same statement it sets `Escalated`, and new alert ids embed a nanosecond timestamp so they never collide), and `status.Escalated` is persisted forever with no pruning/expiry, so a long-running `ralph org watch` process grows `watch-status-<org_id>.json` without bound; (4) (added cycle-3, was self-review cycle-2 finding L2-6) the AC-5 zero-active-seats guard (`evaluateTotalBudget`, `internal/org/watch.go:548-559`) returns early and leaves `watchConditionRecord.Active` untouched once every seat in an org ends, so a later-spawned seat that trips the same total-budget condition is cut off silently: `alreadyAlerted` (derived from the never-cleared `Active` flag) gates the ALERT, and the existing regression test only covers the case where the guard fires before any alert was ever sent.~~ (RESOLVED 2026-08-03 in chore/org-debt-batch) | (1) reduces operator diagnosability of state-dir resolution with no correctness impact; (2) a busy seat's scope-change ALERT can reach Lead with zero actionable content; (3) is dead code (no behavior risk) plus an unbounded-growth concern for very long-running watch processes; (4) a resumed org's first post-resume cutoff produces no ALERT at all, so Lead has no signal that enforcement fired against a seat it may not know is budget-constrained. | Self-review flagged (1)-(3) as LOW ("batchable, no urgency") in cycle 1/2; the in-cycle fix-and-revalidate pass (commit `79d9f40`) closed 4 of the 7 cycle-1 LOW findings but not these three, and bundling them into an already-near-cap pipeline cycle was judged unnecessary churn each time. (4) was flagged in self-review cycle 2 (L2-6) and reconfirmed still open and untracked in cycle 3 — folded into this row per the cycle-3 report's own recommendation rather than left to survive a fourth cycle unregistered. | ~~Next time `internal/org/watch.go` or `internal/cli/org.go`'s watch-banner code is touched for any other reason — apply: print `source` in the watch banner/`status`/`doctor`, truncate `git status --porcelain` output to a bounded head (e.g. first 20 lines + "… N more") before building the scope-change ALERT (with `SEAT` in the fallback), drop/comment the unreachable `Escalated` guard while capping or expiring `Escalated` entries, and either clear `Active` when the zero-active-seats guard fires (so a resumed org re-alerts on its next cutoff) or document in the guard's comment that a resumed cutoff is intentionally silent.~~ | `docs/reports/self-review-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md` (LOW findings, Cycle 2 finding L2-6), `docs/reports/verify-2026-08-02-org-runtime-watchdog.md`, `internal/cli/org.go`, `internal/cli/status.go`, `internal/org/watch.go` (`sendAlert`, `checkDeadman`, `escalateAlert`, `evaluateTotalBudget`), `docs/plans/archive/2026-08-03-org-debt-batch.md` (Slice 4) |

| `PlanCoreReplaceDesired` (`internal/upgrade/replaceplan.go`), the core replace planner behind `ralph upgrade`'s v2 engine (`internal/cli/upgrade_v2.go`), has no rename/move detection: a template path rename between versions (e.g. the pack-rule relocation `.claude/rules/<lang>.md` → `.claude/rules/ralph/<lang>.md`, Phase 2 Slice 4) classifies as an unrelated delete-at-old-path plus create-at-new-path, because classification is purely per-path presence/absence with no cross-path correlation. | A downstream v2 project that upgrades across a template path rename sees one extra remove + one extra add in its upgrade report instead of a single clean rename; no data loss (both ops classify correctly on their own), but the report is noisier than the actual change. | Rename/move detection was out of scope for both Phase 2 (which introduced the relocation this row originally cited) and Phase 3 (which wired the planner into `internal/cli` without changing its classification model); adding it is a planner-level design change, not incidental to either phase's file list. | Next template path rename that ships in a release, or the next time `PlanCoreReplaceDesired`'s classification logic is touched for any other reason — add path-rename detection (e.g. correlate a delete/create pair by content hash) so the report collapses them into a single rename op. | `internal/upgrade/replaceplan.go` (`PlanCoreReplaceDesired`), `internal/cli/language_pack.go` (`packRuleRelPath`), `docs/plans/archive/2026-08-17-overlay-scaffold-v2-p2.md` (Slice 4, Slice 6), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-08-18-overlay-scaffold-v2-p3.md` (M4, row reconciliation) |

<!-- RESOLVED 2026-10-07 in fix/guard-deny-only (Slice 2, commit e48797a2): the guard now reads the command with an awk lexer instead of matching substrings, and the recommended form passes: `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'`, a newline, the body, a line that is only `EOF`, then `)"` (also with `<<"EOF"` and `<<\EOF`), because a quoted delimiter expands nothing. Any message that holds `$(` or a backtick outside that exact shape is still denied (`git commit -m "$(id)"`, `-am "$(id)"`, `-m"$(id)"`, `--message "$(id)"`, and a `git commit -F -` fed an unquoted-delimiter heredoc whose body has `$(`). Heredoc bodies with a quoted delimiter are data, so an example string inside a body no longer makes the guard react. `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` pins the passing form and the denied near-misses on both the jq and jq-absent paths. Three remainders, all listed in the guard-limits row below: when awk is missing or exits non-zero, the four substring rules of the old guard decide and still deny the HEREDOC form; since 4e829e34 a backslash-newline anywhere in the command drops every data region, so the HEREDOC form is denied when any line of the command ends in a backslash; and since a9ef82b1 a data region exists only when every top-level command of the Bash call starts with a data command or git, so the HEREDOC form is denied when the same call also runs another command (`make test && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'` and `./scripts/run-verify.sh && git add a.txt && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'`), while it passes as a command of its own and after `git add a.txt &&`; `git commit -F <file>` and `git commit -F -` fed a quoted heredoc pass after any command. Row preserved for traceability. -->
| ~~`.claude/hooks/pre_bash_guard.sh` のコミットメッセージガードが、リポジトリ自身が `git-commit-strategy.md` で推奨する安全形(単一引用 HEREDOC を `-m` に渡す形)を誤検知して deny する — ダブルクォート内の command-substitution 文字列を一律に弾くため、シェル展開されない安全なパターンと危険な生リテラルを区別できない。本行の初回記録時も、追記コマンドが `git commit -m` を含む複合コマンドだったため heredoc 本文中の例示文字列にガードが反応し、python 経由の書き込みを要した(同一の過剰マッチングの実例)。2026-08-20 の 2 つの実装セッションが連続して踏み、`git commit -F <file>` / 単一行 `-m` への回避を強いられた。~~ (RESOLVED 2026-10-07 in fix/guard-deny-only — 推奨の HEREDOC の形は通る。残るのは、awk がない環境の代替規則、コマンドのどこかの行末に `\` がある場合の deny(4e829e34 以降)、同じ Bash 呼び出しに読むだけのコマンドでも `git` でもないコマンドがある場合の deny(a9ef82b1 以降。`make test &&` のあとの推奨の形が当たる。`git commit` を単独で実行するか `-F <ファイル>` を使う)。いずれもガードの限界の行を参照) | 推奨コミット形式が使えず、エージェントごとに場当たり回避が発生する(ガード自体の信頼性低下)。安全性の欠陥ではない(過剰検知の側)。 | 発見が別プランのパイプライン進行中で、hooks 変更はスコープ外だったため。2026-10-07 の guard-bypass-and-hygiene も `pre_bash_guard.sh` に触れたが(bypass で ask を返さない、`.git`・`.env` の ask を書き込み先だけにする、deny を ask より先に判定する、jq なしの取り出しがエスケープを読む)、この行の誤検知は plan の Non-goals に入れて意図的に残した。HEREDOC の形は今も deny になる。 | ~~`.claude/hooks/pre_bash_guard.sh`(+templates twin)への次のタッチのうち、コミットメッセージの規則(ダブルクォートの `-m` の中のコマンド置換の deny)を直すもの(2026-10-07 の guard-bypass-and-hygiene の接触はこの規則を変えていないので、きっかけには数えない) — 単一引用 HEREDOC 由来の command substitution を許容するか、`git-commit-strategy.md` の推奨形を `-F <file>` ベースに改める(どちらかに統一)。fixture テスト必須(tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh)。~~ — RESOLVED: 前者(単一引用 HEREDOC 由来の置換を許容)で統一した。推奨形は変えていない。`tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` が通る形と deny の形を固定している | 2026-08-20 の実装セッション報告 ×2、`.claude/hooks/pre_bash_guard.sh`、`.claude/rules/ralph/git-commit-strategy.md`、`tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` |

| `checkCodexAgmsgWritableRoot` (doctor's "Codex sandbox (agmsg writable root)" check, #164) reads only the user-level codex config (`$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`, else `~/.codex/config.toml`) — codex profiles, a project-level `.codex/config.toml`, and `-c` overrides are never read; a configured `profile` only earns a "not evaluated" note in the Detail (`codexWritableRootSuffix`), not a value the check actually consults. Four further limits are unverified rather than by-design: whether codex expands `~` or a relative path in `writable_roots` is unconfirmed, so `pathCovers` treats any non-absolute root as never covering — a deliberate false-warn bias, not a confirmed true negative; codex documents that `.git`, `.agents`, and `.codex` directories under a writable root are read-only recursively but not whether that applies only to a root's top-level entries, so `pathCrossesCodexProtectedDir` rejects a root when any path element between it and the store has one of those names — the same false-warn bias; the claim that a guarded codex seat inherits the user config's own `sandbox_mode` is inferred from `permissionArgsForDriver` returning no sandbox flag for guarded seats (`codexSandboxReasons`'s reason (b)), not live-verified against a running guarded codex seat; and `$TMPDIR` / `AGMSG_STORAGE_PATH` are read from doctor's own process environment (`codexSandboxEnvFromOS`), not the herdr seat's pane — the Detail names this only when the value actually drove the result (`codexWritableRootSuffix`'s override note, `codexImplicitRootDetail`'s `$TMPDIR` note), not when doctor's own environment lacks a value the pane's has. Since #170, a store inside the project narrows the warn's Detail (a seat whose working directory contains the store can already write it) but does not flip the outcome to `pass`: the check cannot know a codex seat's actual `--cwd` (a seat may run in a task worktree, e.g. `.claude/worktrees/<slug>`, whose working directory does not contain a store that lives elsewhere in the project), so treating an in-project store as automatically covered would risk a false pass for such a seat. | A project that relies on a profile, a project-level `.codex/config.toml`, a `-c sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=…` override, or a `~`/relative `writable_roots` entry codex does expand, still gets a `warn` from this check even though the real seat can write to the agmsg store — a false positive (misleading warn), not a missed real gap. | Reproducing codex's full config-layering stack (project merge order, profile selection, `-c` overrides) is explicitly out of scope per the plan's Non-goals, which scoped the check to one named file rather than codex's own resolution; confirming `~`/relative expansion and guarded-seat `sandbox_mode` inheritance needs a live codex seat run, which the plan's Non-goals also rule out (`docs/evidence/codex-seat-permissions-2026-09-18.md` Run A2 proved root-present/absent behavior only for an absolute root); reading the herdr pane's own environment instead of doctor's would need doctor to inspect a running seat's process, which the plan's Non-goals also rule out. | Next touch to `internal/cli/doctor_codex_writable_root.go` for another reason, a live codex confirmation of `~`/relative expansion in `writable_roots`, or the first operator report of a warn on a project that relies on a profile / project-level config / `-c` override / pane-only `$TMPDIR` or `AGMSG_STORAGE_PATH` the check cannot see. | `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-19-codex-agmsg-writable-root.md` (Non-goals, Assumptions), `internal/cli/doctor_codex_writable_root.go` (`pathCovers`, `codexWritableRootSuffix`, `codexSandboxReasons`, `codexSandboxEnvFromOS`, `codexImplicitRootDetail`), `docs/recipes/codex-seat-permissions.md` |
| `checkShellAliases` (doctor's "Shell aliases (codex/claude)" check) grades a shell-alias finding by flag class only — it never reads `ralph.toml`, so it cannot know which seats are actually `guarded`, nor whether `[org.permissions].codex_verified` is really `true` on this project even though its codex clauses now name that gate in prose. `permissionArgsForDriver` returns no flags for `guarded` seats on either driver, so a guarded seat with a `--sandbox`/`--ask-for-approval` (codex) or `--permission-mode` (claude) alias runs with the alias's value unopposed; the Detail sentences and the mirrored doc prose stay conditional ("… once codex_verified = true … a guarded seat runs with the alias's value …") instead of naming what happens on this project's own config. Three further limits are permanent, by design or by an unrepresentable case, not by an easy fix: files pulled in via `source` are not followed (`shellAliasRcCandidates`'s static candidate list); aliases defined dynamically (shell functions, `eval`) are invisible to a line-based static scan (`checkShellAliases` never launches an interactive shell — see the plan's Non-goals); and an unbalanced quote that a *later*, unrelated alias statement's own quote happens to close swallows the lines in between with no `not fully parsed` signal (`shellAliasFileScan.Unclosed`'s doc comment). | An operator who has already set `codex_verified = true` and assigned a `guarded` role in `[org.permissions].roles` gets no configuration-specific warning; they must re-derive from the conditional wording whether their own setup is affected. Not a correctness bug in the check's own severity math (self-review N1 and cycle-2 C2-4 already fixed the sentences so they no longer overstate the mechanism) — a missed opportunity to be concrete. | Config-aware grading needs `checkShellAliases` to parse `ralph.toml` (or accept a resolved `[org.permissions]` view) and cross the flag class against each configured role's mode, which is a second data source and a larger surface than the static rc-scan this PR shipped; self-review N1 and cycle-2 C2-4 both recommended a wording fix for their own cycle and left the config-aware version as a tech-debt row. | Next touch to `internal/cli/doctor_shell_alias.go` for another reason, or the first operator report of a guarded seat silently running with an alias-widened sandbox/permission mode despite a `pass`/`info` doctor result for their own config. #167 (fix/doctor-shell-alias-rc-types, 2026-09-25) fired the first half of this trigger — FIFO/non-regular rc handling and relative `$ZDOTDIR` resolution — without taking on the debt itself; the plan's Non-goals explicitly kept severity rules unchanged. Remaining trigger: the next change to `checkShellAliases`'s severity `switch`, or to how `ralph` resolves/passes `[org.permissions]` flags to a driver, or the operator report above. | `docs/reports/self-review-2026-09-18-doctor-shell-alias-check.md` (N1, Cycle 2 C2-4), `docs/reports/cross-review-triage-doctor-shell-alias-check.md`, `internal/cli/doctor_shell_alias.go` (`checkShellAliases`, `shellAliasRcCandidates`, `shellAliasFileScan.Unclosed`), `internal/org/permissions.go` (`permissionArgsForDriver`), `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-18-doctor-shell-alias-check.md` (Non-goals) |

| `scripts/secret-scan.sh`'s range scan shares two blind spots with CI's own `.github/workflows/verify.yml` invocation, none closable by local git-config pinning alone: a committed `-diff`/`binary` `.gitattributes` attribute or a real binary file, where `git log -p` prints "Binary files differ" and never scans the content; and a merge commit, where `git log -p` prints no diff by default so a secret introduced while resolving a conflict is never scanned | A secret committed via a binary file or a merge-conflict resolution passes both CI and every local scan silently | Explicit Non-goals in `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (#176); the `-diff`/binary and merge-commit gaps are CI's own `git log -p` invocation shape, not fixable by `-c`/options on the local scanner alone without a matching CI change | Next change to `scan_range`/`scan_diff_stream`, or a policy decision to also scan binary files/merge commits (needs a matching CI change first, since local must not scan more than CI does) | `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Non-goals), `docs/reports/verify-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` |
| Local-only ways `secret-scan.sh`'s range scan can still say clean while CI reports a finding. `secret-scan-branch.sh`'s own git calls (`merge-base`, `rev-list`, the `.gitallowed` read, and the attribute-source calls `git diff` and `git merge-tree`) still follow `refs/replace/*`, so a local replace ref for HEAD can supply a `.gitallowed` rule CI never sees; exporting `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` (the environment form of the `--no-replace-objects` that `scan_range` already passes) closes it. A git older than 2.41 ignores `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, so there a range ending at HEAD reads the working tree's attributes, and an uncommitted `.gitattributes` edit can hide a file. The range scan reads `.git/info/attributes` as git does, so the merge guard (`prepare-commit-msg-secret-guard.sh`, `HEAD..<merge head>`) can pass a merge whose token a `-diff` rule there hides; only `secret-scan-branch.sh` refuses to scan while that file holds a rule, which keeps an unrelated rule from blocking every local non-fast-forward merge. While `.git/info/attributes` holds a rule, `secret-scan-branch.sh`'s default mode (`run-verify.sh`'s early warning) prints "cannot scan" and exits 0 without scanning, where main's script scanned with that file; `/pr --strict` still stops with exit 3 (#181's AC-3 decision; trigger: the next touch to `check_info_attributes`). (Pinned or stopped: a local `diff.<driver>.binary` for a driver the committed `.gitattributes` names is set to `auto` for every key git config lists, a driver name holding `.` or `=` included, so git decides from the content as it does in CI; the system-wide attributes file is dropped with `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`, and the user-level one with `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`; a rule line in `.git/info/attributes`, which no setting stops git from reading, makes `secret-scan-branch.sh` stop with "cannot scan". For a range ending at HEAD on git 2.41 or later, an uncommitted working-tree `.gitattributes` edit is pinned by `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, and a local `attr.tree` by `-c attr.tree=`; a range ending elsewhere, the merge guard's `HEAD..<merge head>`, reads the working tree's attributes, which during a merge are the merge result's, as main's scanner did.) | A local `secret-scan-branch.sh --strict` can print clean right before push while the same range fails in CI (replace refs, git older than 2.41); the merge guard can pass a merge that CI later flags (`.git/info/attributes`) | The replace ref reproduced in #176's cycle-2 work (clean with a replaced HEAD, findings again with `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`) and sits in `secret-scan-branch.sh`'s own git calls, outside #181's attribute scope; the working-tree read before git 2.41 is the floor of `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` itself. The merge guard reads `.git/info/attributes` by #181's cycle-1 decision: its refusal blocked a real `git merge` over an unrelated rule, and the push gate (`/pr --strict`) and CI still read the branch before it merges | Next touch to `secret-scan-branch.sh`'s git calls or to the merge guard, or the first real report of a local-clean/CI-finding mismatch traced to one of these causes | `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Deviation notes, work slice), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Cycle 2), `docs/reports/test-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md`, `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-27-secret-scan-attr-parity.md` (#181), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-09-27-secret-scan-attr-parity.md` |
| CI's `pull_request` checkout is the PR merge commit (`refs/pull/<n>/merge`). When the base changed `.gitattributes` after the branch forked, `secret-scan-branch.sh` reads the attributes of a local `git merge-tree --write-tree <base> HEAD`, but that local merge is not GitHub's: a local base ref older than the remote's gives a different merge. Which committed attributes GitHub's server-side merge reads is undocumented: vanilla git in a bare repository reads no in-tree attributes by default, while this script uses HEAD's committed ones, and the two differ only when a committed `.gitattributes` gives `.gitattributes` itself a `merge=` attribute (`union` or `binary`). (merge-tree itself runs with git's default merge settings, `merge.renormalize=false`, `merge.renames=true`, `merge.directoryRenames=conflict`, `merge.renameLimit=7000`, and with HEAD's committed attributes only: `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` set to HEAD's commit, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1`, `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null`, `-c attr.tree=`, so a local attributes file, renormalization with a clean filter, or a working-tree or inherited `merge=union` cannot change how `.gitattributes` merges.) Documented behavior, not debt: a conflicting merge reads HEAD's attributes with a notice (CI does not run on a conflicting pull request); where the merge cannot be computed the way CI's is (a local `merge.default` or `merge.<name>.driver`, a git older than 2.41, a merge-tree without `--write-tree` or failing), `--strict` exits 3 and default mode reads HEAD's attributes with a notice, and both name the remedy (merge or rebase the base into the branch so HEAD carries its `.gitattributes`); a direct `secret-scan.sh --range` call reads HEAD's attributes unless the caller sets `RALPH_SECRET_SCAN_ATTR_SOURCE` | A local `secret-scan-branch.sh --strict` scan can read other attributes than CI's merge commit when the base ref was not fetched recently | The base ref (origin first) is the one the merge-base already uses, so a stale fetch already shifts the scanned range itself; #181's cycle-1 cross-review (reviewed HEAD af29652) reproduced a local renormalization changing the merged attributes, which the merge-tree pins now prevent | Next touch to `use_merge_attributes` in `secret-scan-branch.sh`, or the first PR whose CI secret scan fails after a clean local `--strict` scan | `docs/plans/archive/2026-09-27-secret-scan-attr-parity.md` (#181), `docs/reports/cross-review-triage-secret-scan-attr-parity.md` as of the cycle-1 triage (reviewed HEAD af29652; the file is rewritten each cycle, so read it at d884024), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Cycle 3, C3-L3) |
| Parts of #176's scanner changes that no test discriminates, plus one documented `--diff` limit: `-c log.showSignature=false` (the cycle-1 self-review noted the flag it replaced, `--no-show-signature`, as untested) and `--no-ext-diff` (proved this cycle to be a structural no-op for `scan_range`'s exact `git log -p` invocation — git only invokes an external diff driver when `--ext-diff` is also passed, so no fixture can discriminate it); the `HUP`/`INT` signal traps (only `TERM` has a dedicated test, though the code is the same three-line shape for all three signals); the SGR strip in `scan_diff_stream` — not just its stacked-code loop but its anchoring entirely — is unexercised: replacing it outright with the original unanchored, global strip (the pre-AR-1-fix shape) still passes every test, because the `in_hunk` state machine the AR-1 fix added is what actually prevents a `+++ `-shaped hunk-content line from being mis-skipped, independent of how the SGR strip itself is written; and `--diff` given a multi-file unified diff without a `diff ` line per file (concatenated `diff -u` output, svn-style `Index:` sections), where a later file's `+++ ` header arrives inside the previous hunk and is read as content, so a token-shaped file name is reported (an over-report only; the usage text says `--diff` expects git-style input, and no caller in the repo uses `--diff`). Separately, the `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` comment in `scan_range` states git 2.41+: the git 2.41.0 release notes introduce `--attr-source`, and in containers git 2.40.4 ignores the variable while git 2.43.7 honors it, but 2.41 and 2.42 themselves were not run. Also, `A...B` ranges have a disclosed residual, now concretely demonstrated rather than only described: when the resolved end `B` equals HEAD, `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` pins every commit the range shows — including ones reachable only from `A` — to `B`'s tree, so a commit on `A`'s own side is read with `B`'s attributes instead of its own; the two `A...B`-named tests pin this documented behavior by name without exercising the actual miss it can cause | A future accidental removal of `-c log.showSignature=false`, the `HUP`/`INT` trap lines, or the SGR strip would not be caught by the test suite; a non-git `--diff` caller can get a false positive on a file name; a mis-stated git-version floor would go unnoticed until an actual pre-2.41 git ran the range scan; an `A...B` caller with divergent per-branch attributes could miss a secret on the non-HEAD side — low risk, since no caller in the repo passes `A...B` (`verify.yml`, `secret-scan-branch.sh`, and `prepare-commit-msg-secret-guard.sh` all pass plain `A..B`) | `docs/reports/test-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` judged the fixture cost of testing `HUP`/`INT` (tripling that section's setup) not worth it for a narrow, low-severity gap, re-confirmed unchanged in cycle 3; `--no-ext-diff` needs no test since it is provably unreachable for this invocation; `log.showSignature=false` was explicitly accepted untested in self-review; the SGR strip's anchoring was first flagged narrower (the stacked-code loop only) by the cycle-2 self-review, then broadened by cycle-2 `/test`'s mutation 3 to the strip's shape entirely, and re-confirmed unchanged in cycle 3; the `--diff` input limit was found by the cycle-2 self-review in the final pipeline cycle, and counting hunk lines from the `@@ -a,b +c,d @@` header (which would recognize a header without a `diff ` line) is a parser change beyond that cycle; the `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` version floor is bounded by the release notes and cycle-3 `/test`'s 2.40.4/2.43.7 container runs, without a 2.41 or 2.42 run; the `A...B` residual was disclosed by self-review run 3 (C3-L1) and concretely demonstrated by cycle-3 `/test`, which judged it low-priority and untested-by-name since no caller exists | Next touch to `scan_range`'s pinned options, the signal-trap block, or `scan_diff_stream`; or the first `--diff` caller that passes non-git input; a git 2.41 or 2.42 run of the attribute-source tests; or the first caller that passes an `A...B` range | `docs/reports/test-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Coverage, Test gaps, Cycle 2 Gaps, Cycle 3 Gaps sections), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-09-25-secret-scan-git-config.md` (Cycle 2, Cycle 3 C3-L1) |

| `ralph org` verbs resolve the config and the state dir by different rules. Config: `--config`, else only the cwd's `./ralph.toml`, else the built-in default (`resolveOrgConfig` in `internal/cli/org.go`). State dir: `--state-dir`, else `RALPH_ORG_STATE_DIR`, else the main worktree's root (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel` inside the main worktree, the first record of `git worktree list --porcelain` inside a linked worktree), else the git toplevel (when no main worktree can be named, such as a bare repository's worktree), else the cwd (`ResolveOrgStateDir` in `internal/org/statedir.go`; the main-worktree tier arrived with `org-state-dir-common`). Run from a repo subdirectory without `--config`, a verb silently uses the built-in default config against the repo's real state dir (self-review probe C: `ralph org status` exited 0 and listed `seat-1` while ignoring the project's `ralph.toml`). The gap widened with `org-state-dir-common`: run from the root of a linked worktree, the config is that worktree's own `./ralph.toml` (a branch can carry a different `[org]` section) while the ledger is the main worktree's, so the envelope is checked against seats that other checkouts started (reasoned from `envelope.go`'s `max_seats` check against the ledger's active seats, not exercised). Separately, every verb, the teardown verbs `status`/`stop`/`disband` included, goes through `newOrgRuntime` → `resolveOrgConfig`, so any `ralph.toml` validation error (for example an `[org.roles]` model that is no longer in the effective `model_pool`) blocks stopping seats that are still running | From a subdirectory, a verb can apply the wrong envelope (`model_pool`, `[org.roles]`, `max_seats`) to a live org with no warning. A broken `ralph.toml` leaves running seats reachable only after the file is fixed or a fixed copy is passed with `--config`. Mitigated, not removed: the `/org` skill's "既定の model_pool" section now documents the recovery (fix `ralph.toml`, or pass `--config`; the state dir does not depend on the config's location), and `TestOrgStatus_ConfigFlagRecoversAfterDefaultPoolDropsRoleModel` pins that path. Since org-limits-reserve, `spawn` and `start` read `max_orgs` and `max_total_seats` from the main worktree's `ralph.toml` when the ledger is the main worktree's and `--config` is absent (`withMainWorktreeOrgLimits` in `internal/cli/org.go`), so those two keys no longer follow the cwd's file; `max_seats`, `model_pool`, `[org.roles]`, and the teardown verbs still do. The cost is one more stuck case: a main `ralph.toml` that fails to load stops `spawn` and `start` from a linked worktree even when that worktree's own file is valid (`TestOrgStart_MainWorktreeRalphTomlLoadError`); `--config` avoids it, and so does a `--state-dir` or `RALPH_ORG_STATE_DIR` naming another ledger; one naming the main worktree's own ledger does not, because since feat/org-feature-worktree (commit 07d38e6d) `withMainWorktreeOrgLimits` reads the main worktree's `ralph.toml` for it too (so that a feature org's leader, which always passes `--state-dir`, cannot change the org-wide limits with its branch's file), and a main `ralph.toml` that fails to load then also stops that leader's `spawn` (reasoned from the code, not exercised) | Predates `drop-gpt-5-5-default-pool`, which only surfaced it. That plan's Non-goals excluded loosening the teardown verbs' config validation because it widens scope past a one-slug default change, so it chose docs plus a regression test. `org-state-dir-common` (2026-10-07) changed `ResolveOrgStateDir` and re-judged this row: its Scope is where the ledger lives, not how config is found, and widening it to config discovery was not planned, so the row stays open | A report of a wrong-envelope or stuck-teardown incident, or any change to `resolveOrgConfig`, `withMainWorktreeOrgLimits`, or `MainWorktreeRoot`, or a further change to `ResolveOrgStateDir` (the open part is that config discovery still ignores the main worktree and the git toplevel, which the state dir now follows) (for example searching up to the git toplevel for `ralph.toml`, or letting the teardown verbs fall back to the built-in default with a warning on a validation error). Same shape as (b) in the `checkCodexAgmsgWritableRoot` row above (`runDoctorFull(".")` does no upward search), so one project-root discovery helper could fix both. `withMainWorktreeOrgLimits` changed in feat/org-feature-worktree (commit 07d38e6d: a `--state-dir` or `RALPH_ORG_STATE_DIR` naming the main worktree's ledger now reads the main worktree's file, see the Impact), which met that part of this trigger and left the open part as it was | `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-02-drop-gpt-5-5-default-pool.md` (Non-goals, Deviation notes), `docs/reports/verify-2026-10-02-drop-gpt-5-5-default-pool.md` (D-2), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-10-02-drop-gpt-5-5-default-pool.md` (probe C, Follow-ups), `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-07-org-state-dir-common.md` (Scope), `docs/reports/verify-2026-10-07-org-state-dir-common.md` (D-1), `docs/reports/verify-2026-10-08-org-limits-reserve.md` (V-8), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-10-09-org-feature-worktree.md` (cycle 2: C2-6 (b)) |

| Residual risks of `/cross-review`'s codex reviewer and `/plan`'s Codex advisory that the read-only sandbox and `--ignore-rules` (issue #197) do not cover. (a) MCP servers from the user-level config still start in the reviewer and advisory sessions, and their tools run in the server process, outside the sandbox (whether the model can call them under `approval: never` is unverified). `-c 'mcp_servers={}'` does not remove them; `-c mcp_servers.<name>.enabled=false` per server or `--ignore-user-config` does (probe R12). (b) The read-only sandbox does not stop reads: the reviewer can read any file the user can, and whatever it repeats in its output ends up in `docs/reports/cross-review-triage-*.md`, which is committed and pushed (probe R13). (c) The reverse direction's claude reviewer (`claude -p --permission-mode auto`) has not been reviewed for the same class of issue. (d) `ralph doctor --probe-models` runs `codex exec --model <m> --skip-git-repo-check ping` with no sandbox override (`internal/org/driver/probe.go:26`), so in a trusted project it inherits the project's `sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"`. The prompt is the fixed word `ping`, so no reviewed text reaches it and the risk is low; it is recorded for completeness. (e) `tests/test-codex-exec-invocation.sh` reads one physical line the way sh splits it into words, so these edits pass it: a backslash-newline continuation (only the first physical line of an invocation is scanned); an invocation that does not start a simple command with `command codex …` (subshell, brace group, bare or absolute `codex`, `env`, `sh -c`, `eval`, `time`, `if`); a `#` comment that hides flags; and a shell expansion that yields an option (`$(...)`, backticks, `${}`, brace expansion, `$'..'`, glob). Each takes a deliberate edit, and the `#` form also comments out the launch line's redirect, watchdog, and `echo "codex rc="`, so the skill visibly stops working. A `-c mcp_servers.<name>.command=…` at the root passes every check too and starts a program outside the sandbox while the run header still reads `sandbox: read-only` (same class as (a)); a `-c notify=[…]` passes the same way, but whether codex runs it is unverified | A user whose config has an MCP server that can write files or call out could see the reviewer act through it on an instruction hidden in the diff. A secret the reviewer reads and repeats reaches the remote unless the shape-based secret scan catches it. An edit of the (e) kind can drop the read-only selector or `--ignore-rules` from a codex call while the test stays green | Issue #197 was scoped to the command sandbox and execpolicy rules. Turning MCP off needs each server's name (`enabled=false`) or `--ignore-user-config`, which also drops the rest of the user config, such as custom model providers and the trust entry that makes codex load the project `.codex/config.toml`, so it is a maintainer decision. (c) concerns a different CLI. (e) is adversarial-only: closing it means either modelling more of sh or rejecting the characters the tokenizer does not model, and the test already catches the realistic regressions (a late `-c`, a dropped or widened sandbox word, a missing `--ignore-rules`) | The next change to the codex calls of `/cross-review` or `/plan`; a codex release that documents how `codex exec` approves MCP tool calls; a report of a reviewer acting through an MCP tool; the first review of the claude reviewer's permission mode; the next change to `internal/org/driver/probe.go`; or the next change to `tests/test-codex-exec-invocation.sh`, where (e) can be closed by rejecting `$`, backtick, `{`, `(`, `#`, and a trailing backslash on invocation lines (apart from `$BASE` and the `${RALPH_…}` defaults) or at least listed in the test's header comment | Issue #197, `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-03-cross-review-codex-read-only.md` (Open questions, Deviation notes), `docs/reports/self-review-2026-10-03-cross-review-codex-read-only.md` (M-2, probes R12, R13), `internal/org/driver/probe.go:26` (d), `docs/reports/test-2026-10-03-cross-review-codex-read-only.md` (G-2 to G-6 and the Slice D addendum, for (e)) |
