(b) A cap-raised extra `/cross-review` pass does not increment `cycle-count.json`, so `--cycle auto` records the same `cycle` for the pass before and after it; `ralph insights` then holds two events for one cycle and keeps the later one. | (a) ~~A warning-level defect added to the script reaches `main` unlinted, while the shellcheck gate reports green.~~ (resolved) (b) The per-cycle verdict history in `ralph insights` cannot tell an extra pass from the pass it follows. | (a) Found while adding `--cycle auto` and outside that change's scope (the PR is about the cycle value, not the lint list). (b) Behavior already confirmed in PR #203 and kept as is; changing it means changing what `/cross-review` step 9 writes to `cycle-count.json`. | (a) ~~The next change to `scripts/insights-append.sh` or to the shellcheck list in `verify.local.sh`.~~ (resolved) (b) The first report from `ralph insights` that misreads a cap-raised pass. | docs/plans/archive/2026-10-06-plan-visual-followups.md (archived by `/pr`; 進捗メモと Design decisions); docs/reports/self-review-2026-10-06-plan-visual-followups.md; docs/plans/archive/2026-10-07-guard-bypass-and-hygiene.md (resolves (a); archived by `/pr`) |
| `pre_bash_guard.sh` (deny-only since fix/guard-deny-only) still decides some commands wrongly, or cannot decide them. The ask rules are gone, and with them the write-form gaps this row used to list (`cp`, `mv`, `install`, and `sed -i` into `.git` or `.env`, the `tee` argument scan, `>&`, `$(...)` and quoted-space targets, tabs on the sed path); the deny spellings it listed (`git commit -am "$(id)"`, `-m"$(id)"`, `--message "$(id)"`, and `sudo` followed by a tab) are denied now (section B of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh`, the `ac2` array). What remains, each probed against the guard on 2026-10-08 at e5c9e6be, except the forms of the cap-4 run (printf, rg, `test`, `[`, and the `--no-verify` rule), which were probed at 6b1f7acc, and the 29 forms of (a) and (b) that carry a stated decision, which were run again there and gave the decisions written here (6b1f7acc changed only the header comment, so its decisions are those of 12e9a9ad, the last commit that changed them; the guard is cited by function and variable names, which `grep` finds, because every line number this row carried went stale each time the guard changed: S2c took the file from 991 to 1286 lines, and the fixes after it, 46806dc9, 4e829e34, the allowlist a9ef82b1, 849f5411, and in the cap-4 run b3c3fdaa, 12e9a9ad, and the header comment of 6b1f7acc, took it to 1461): (a) False positives, from two checks. The wrapper scan: a command that is not in `NOEXEC` (built from `noexec_list`) has its later words read too (`scan_words`, called from `judge`), and a word that is exactly `sudo` with another word after it is denied even when it is data: `apt-get remove sudo -y`, `apt-get install sudo vim`, `bash -c 'x' sudo ls` (the words after the `-c` string are positional parameters of that script), and `flock l git grep sudo file` (`sudo` is the search pattern of `git grep`). The sentinel (`sentinel`: the four substring rules of the previous guard, run on the whole command): a match is ignored only inside a data region, which is the arguments of a command in `DATACMD` (echo, printf, cat, grep, ls, ...; `test`, `[`, and `stat` are not in it, and `printf` and `rg` count only under the conditions given after the allowlist below) whose pipeline holds only such commands and writes no file, a `git commit` or `git tag` `-m` value, the body of a heredoc fed to one of those commands, or a comment. A `${…}` span is no data region either, since 139d6627 (zsh evaluates the value of `${(e)…}` again; `xnote` in `lex_dollar` and `HSUB` in `lex_hd`), but the text around it, the recommended commit HEREDOC form, and the body of a heredoc with a quoted delimiter stay data. Since 1e032dea two more things are no data region. One is a commit or tag message word that holds a `$` that expands (`msg_check` reads the per-word mark `WEXP`, which `lex_dollar` sets, not the `${` in the source text, so a `${` in single quotes, escaped as `\${`, or in `$'…'` does not count). The other is a zsh subscript in the arguments of a data command, from its `$` (`$arr[`, `$~arr[`, `$[`, and since 43e73568 `$@[` and `$#x[`) to the end of its word, which `lex_word` takes out with `xnote`, because zsh evaluates the subscript and runs a `$(…)` written there in single quotes. Since a9ef82b1 (the allowlist, added in the third pipeline run, after the cap was raised to 3) a data region needs one more condition: every top-level simple command of the call must have, as its first word with the quotes removed and read before assignments and wrappers are skipped, a bare `DATACMD` name or `git` with no `/` in it (`data_first_ok`, called from `end_cmd`). Three commands carry a condition of their own, set in the cap-4 run (`stage_note`, `datacmd_list`): `printf` is a data command only when no word starts with `-v` and no word as written holds a `%`, a `$`, or a backtick, because in zsh a `%n` conversion assigns to, and a numeric one such as `%d` evaluates, an argument as an arithmetic expression that runs a subscript such as `arr[$(cmd)]`, and `printf -v` stores into a variable in bash and zsh; `rg` only when no word starts with `--pre` (which runs a program on each file) and no word has a `$` that expands (`$x`, `"$x"`, `$1`, `${…}`), a substitution, or ANSI-C or locale quoting (`$'…'`, `$"…"`), because the value of such a word could be `--pre` (since 1e032dea, read from the marks of `lex_dollar`, so the `$` in `rg 'foo$' 'sudo ' .` does not count); and `test` and `[` are not in `DATACMD` at all, because `-v` in zsh and bash 5 evaluates a subscript the same way (cross-review cycle 3 and self-review C4-1 ran each of these in zsh 5.9 or bash 5.2 with a harmless stand-in for the substitution; not repeated in this sync). `stat` is not in `DATACMD` either (since 139d6627): zsh's `stat -A NAME`, with the zsh/stat module loaded, reads NAME as an array name and evaluates its subscript, as `-v` does (`stat -A 'arr[$(sudo id; echo 1)]' /dev/null`; run in `zsh -f` 5.9 with a harmless stand-in for the substitution, test report of guard-zsh-data-gaps). The printf check reads the words as written, and the rg check reads the marks that `lex_dollar` sets from the source text, not their decoded values, because the lexer decodes only `\n`, `\t`, and `\r` in `$'…'`: `$'\x25n'` is `%n` to zsh and `x25n` to the lexer. Four cases have no data region at all, so the four rules read the whole command as plain text: an allowlist miss, which is any other first word (an assignment, a wrapper such as `env`, `command`, `nice`, `builtin`, or `exec`, a path, a variable or a substitution) or a command of only redirections (`NODATA`, set in `end_cmd`); it includes a compound command that starts with a reserved word such as `if`, `for`, or `case`, or with the `{` of a brace group, and an `exec` with a redirection, because their first word is neither a data command nor `git` (`data_first_ok`; since refactor/guard-debt-cleanup, d7dac506, `end_cmd` has no rule of its own for these two); a heredoc delimiter word that holds a `$` or a backtick (`NODATA`, set in `lex_redir`); a `(` or `)` at the top level (`NODATA`, set in `lex_cmds`: a subshell `(...)` meets the `(` rule, and a case clause written `x)` also the `)` rule after its pattern); and a backslash-newline anywhere in the command (the first lines of the `END` block, which look at the raw text and ignore quoting). In each case the guard cannot tell where the text of a read-only command ends up: a pipe or a redirection may surround the group, `exec` may have changed a file descriptor, `env -S` or another wrapper may run a later word, a path may be any program, the shell may read a delimiter such as `$'\x45'` as `E` and end the heredoc body on an earlier line, and the shell removes a backslash-newline before it reads the command, which turns `"$\<newline>(id)"` into a substitution in bash and dash. The arguments of any other command are searched as plain text, so these are denied as well: `cp sudo dest`, `apt-get install sudo 2>/dev/null`, `git grep sudo file`, `git push --force-if-includes origin main` (it contains the substring `git push --force`; `git push origin --force-if-includes` passes), and the output of a read-only command piped to a command that is not a data reader (`echo 'sudo ls' \| sed 's/x/y/'`, `grep -r 'sudo ' . \| sort`; the same pipelines ending in `cat` or `grep` pass). The previous guard denied every one of these too. `apt-get install sudo`, where `sudo` is the last word, passes. The no-data-region cases add false positives of their own. The previous guard denied all of them, and the guard at 095af1d7 passed each one; 46806dc9 and 4e829e34 made them deny. `(echo sudo ls)`, `if grep -q 'sudo ' file; then echo ok; fi`, a `grep` inside a `for` loop (`for f in docs/*.md; do grep -n 'git push --force' "$f"; done`), and `(cd docs && grep -rn 'sudo ' .)` are denied, while `cd docs && grep -rn 'sudo ' .` passes. `exec >run.log; echo 'sudo ls'` is denied, and so is `exec echo 'sudo ls'` (it passed at facd295b; since the allowlist its first word, `exec`, is not a data command). `echo 'sudo ls' &>/dev/null` is denied because `redir_safe` never accepts `&>` or `&>>`, even toward `/dev/null`, while `echo 'sudo ls' >/dev/null 2>&1` passes (self-review P2-3). Every form with a backslash-newline is denied too, including those where the two characters are only text, because the rule ignores quoting: `echo 'sudo ls \<newline>x'` (single quotes), `echo "never \<newline>sudo ls"`, the end of a line in the body of a quoted heredoc or in a comment, `echo a\\<newline>echo 'sudo ls'` (after an escaped backslash), a command written over several lines with `\` continuations (`echo 'sudo ls' \<newline>  \| cat`), and the recommended commit form `git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'` ... `EOF` ... `)"` when any line of the message ends in a backslash. The deny reason for that last case tells the agent to use a HEREDOC, which it already does. The same form with no such line passes when `git commit` is a command of its own (see the allowlist cases below). Workaround: write the command without line continuations, or split it; for a commit message, reword the line that ends in a backslash, or pass the message with `git commit -F <file>` (`git commit -F msg.txt` passes). The allowlist adds false positives of its own. The previous guard denied each of them, and the guard at facd295b passed each one (probed on 2026-10-08 at e5c9e6be; the jq and jq-absent paths agree). A read-only command behind a prefix: `env echo 'sudo ls'`, `command echo 'sudo ls'`, `nice grep 'sudo ' f`, `x=1 echo 'sudo ls'`, and zsh's `=echo sudo ls`. A read-only command named by a path, which may be any program: `./echo 'sudo ls'`, `/tmp/x/cat 'sudo ls'`, `/bin/echo 'sudo ls'`. A read-only command in a call that also runs a command that is neither a data command nor `git`: `make test && echo 'sudo ls'` and `python3 x.py; grep -n 'sudo ' f` (`git status && echo 'sudo ls'` and `cd docs && grep -n 'sudo ' f && ls` pass). The recommended commit form in such a call: `make test && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'` ..., `./scripts/run-verify.sh && git add a.txt && git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'` ..., `GIT_EDITOR=true git commit -m "$(cat <<'EOF'` ..., and the form followed by `&& ./scripts/secret-scan-branch.sh --strict` are denied, because the fourth substring rule (the first `-m "` after `git commit` followed by `$(` or a backtick) always matches the form and only the `-m` data region used to hide it. The form passes as a command of its own and after `git add a.txt &&`, and `git commit -F <file>`, `git commit -F - <<'EOF'`, and `git commit -m 'msg'` pass after `make test &&`. Self-review C3-1 chose not to carve the form out of the allowlist, because a definition earlier in the same call can change what the substitution inside the message runs. Workaround: run `git commit` as its own Bash command, or use `git commit -F <file>` when chaining after verification. The deny reason of the commit-message rule says the HEREDOC form passes only when `git commit` is a command of its own; that is narrower than the rule, which also passes the form after `git add a.txt &&` and the `-F -` heredoc after any command (verify V4-1). In the other direction, `my-sudo ls` and `x.sudo ls` were denied by the previous guard and pass now, because the sentinel's word boundary counts a letter, a digit, `_`, `.`, and `-` as part of the word (as it does for `visudo -c`); they run a different command, so nothing gets weaker (verify V-1; the previous guard's deny is pinned in section G of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh`, the new guard's none in section D). Dangerous text in the arguments of a command that is not a data reader is denied as before: `gh pr create --body "$(cat <<'EOF' … EOF\n)"` with `git push --force` in the body; pass the body with `--body-file` (the `/pr` skill does). The cap-4 conditions on `printf`, `rg`, `test`, and `[` add false positives of their own. The previous guard denied each of these too, and the guard at e5c9e6be passed each one (probed on 2026-10-08 at 6b1f7acc; the jq and jq-absent paths agree): `printf '%s\n' 'sudo ls'` (a `%` in a word), `` printf `echo x` 'sudo ls' `` and `printf "$x" 'sudo ls'` (a backtick or a `$`), `[ -n 'sudo ls' ]`, `test -n 'sudo ls'`, `test -n 'git push --force'`, and `rg $"sudo ls" .` (a word in `$"…"` form). Workaround: print the text with `echo` or `cat`, or write the `printf` format without a `%`; `printf 'a\ngit push --force'` and `rg 'sudo ls' .` still pass (the first is in AC3). `printf -v c 'sudo ls'` is denied too, as it was at e5c9e6be. The `--no-verify` rule is new in this PR, so its over-denials are new denies, not regressions: the previous guard had no such rule and passes every command named after this sentence (probed at 6b1f7acc, both paths agree). The rule reads `git commit`, `push`, `merge`, `rebase`, `am`, and `pull`. The scan of merge, rebase, am, and pull does not stop at `--`, because a value-taking option can take `--` as its value (`git merge -m -- --no-verify feature` and `git rebase --onto -- --no-verify main` are denied; the first passed at e5c9e6be), so a message that is the text `--no-verify` is denied as well: `git merge -m --no-verify feature`. `commit_rules` still stops at a bare `--` but reads the value-taking long options (`--trailer`, `--author`, `--cleanup`, and seven more) with their value, also abbreviated, so `git commit --trail -- --no-verify -m fix` is denied (it passed at e5c9e6be). `opt_is` reads any prefix of one or more characters after `--` of the options the guard knows, so an abbreviation that git rejects as ambiguous is denied too: `git push --f origin main`, `git commit --n -m x`, `git merge --n feature`, and `git pull --n origin main` each stop in git 2.49.0 with `ambiguous option` and exit code 129 (scratch repository, 2026-10-08), so the deny stops a command that fails anyway. `git reset --h` is a real deny: git 2.49.0 runs it as a hard reset (the prefix is unique), and the guard passed it until 12e9a9ad. Look-alikes pass: `git merge --no-ff -- feature`, `git pull --no-rebase origin main`, `git push --follow-tags origin main`. Under another git version a one-letter prefix can be unique where it is ambiguous in 2.49.0; whether the guard's value-skipping then disagrees with git was not checked (verify, cycle 4). (b) What the guard cannot see, because the text it reads does not contain it (the "Not covered" paragraph of the header comment lists most of these): a variable (`git commit -m "$msg"`, `$cmd`), an alias, a function, a git alias, a script read from a file (`bash script.sh`, `cat file \| sh`), a remote command (`ssh host 'git push origin --force'` returns no decision; `ssh host 'sudo ls'` is denied by the sentinel, and `ssh host sudo ls` by the wrapper scan too, because `ssh` is not in `NOEXEC`), `core.hooksPath` set beforehand (`git config core.hooksPath /dev/null`; the rule reads only `git -c` and `--config-env` before the subcommand, `git_rules`), and a commit message piped into `git commit -F -` (`echo "$(id)" \| git commit -F -`; only a heredoc or a here-string fed to `-F -` is read). Static shell expansions that the text does show are not followed either: brace expansion (`{su,}do ls`), pathname expansion (`?udo ls`, `[s]udo ls`), and the hex and octal escapes of `$'…'` (`$'\x73udo' ls`; the lexer decodes only `\n`, `\t`, and `\r`); the sentinel sees only the text as written, so it misses them too, as the old guard did. All of these return no decision; the plan's Non-goals exclude them. One escape goes the other way: the lexer reads `$'\echo'` as `echo`, where bash and zsh read ESC and `cho`, so `$'\echo' 'sudo ls'` gets a data region and passes while the previous guard denies it (found in refactor/guard-awk-split, S3; the shell then runs a command named ESC `cho`, which exists only if someone puts one on the `PATH`). A second form goes the same way: `$"echo" 'sudo ls'` gets a data region too, because the lexer and bash read `$"echo"` as `echo`, while zsh and dash read a `$` before it, a command named `$echo` (which also exists only if someone puts one on the `PATH`), so it passes while the previous guard denies it. It also passed at base 0abfede5 (none with and without jq at both 0abfede5 and 1b02beef, probed in this sync), so the split did not add it (self-review LOW 1; commit 8c61c6cb wrote it into the comment of `data_first_ok`). `printf` and `rg` are the exception since 12e9a9ad: they read their words as written, so a hex escape cannot hide `--pre`, a `%n`, `-v`, or a `$` from them. `rg $'\x2d-pre' sh 'sudo ls'`, `printf $'\x25n' $'arr[\x24(sudo id; echo 1)]'`, and `printf $'\x2dv' c 'sudo ls'` are denied (each passed at e5c9e6be and is denied by the previous guard; probed at 6b1f7acc, both paths agree). A variable word is not read that way, and that is a gap of the same kind as `git commit -m "$msg"`: `rg $x sudo pat .` passes at 6b1f7acc (and passed at e5c9e6be), and the previous guard denies it. (Closed in fix/guard-msg-param-flag, commit 1e032dea: `lex_dollar` now sets a per-word mark for a `$` that expands, and rg is left out of the data commands for a word with that mark, with ANSI-C or locale quoting, or with a substitution, so `rg $x sudo pat .`, `rg "$x" 'sudo ' .`, and `rg $(echo --pre) sh 'sudo ls'` are denied, while `rg 'foo$' 'sudo ' .` now passes. The rest of this paragraph describes the state before that commit.) rg is left out of the data commands for a `$'…'` or `$"…"` word, not for a plain `$x`, and if `x` holds `--pre` from outside the call, rg runs the next word as a program on each file (a stand-in program that logged its argument did run with `x=--pre; rg $x ./prog.sh HIT file`), so `sudo` hides in a data region. With the assignment in the same call (`x=--pre; rg $x sudo pat .`, `export x=--pre && rg $x sudo pat .`) the call is denied, because its first word is not a data command. Self-review C4-1 recommended leaving rg out for any word that holds a `$`; 12e9a9ad chose `$'` and `$"` only, and verify and the cycle 4 test report left the choice as it stands, since the content of `$x` is run-time information (the plan's Non-goals). `printf` has no such gap: a `$` in any of its words, as in `printf "$x" 'sudo ls'`, denies. Runners that take their command as one string leave the same gap: `watch 'git push origin --force'`, `watch 'git -C x reset --hard'`, `watch 'git push origin -f'`, `su -c 'git push origin --force'`, `flock /tmp/l -c 'git push origin --force'`, `script -c 'git push origin --force' /dev/null`, `parallel 'git push origin --force' ::: a`, `tmux new-session 'git push origin --force'`, and `find . -exec sh -c 'git push origin --force' \;` return no decision, and so did the previous guard. The wrapper scan compares whole words, so a string that holds a command is not one; a `-c` string is re-read only when `sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `dash`, or `ksh` is the command word (`sh -c 'git push origin --force'` and `xargs -I{} sh -c '…'` are denied), not after `find -exec`, `su`, or `flock`; and the sentinel needs the exact text `git push --force`, `git push -f`, or `git reset --hard`, so `watch 'git push --force'` is denied and `watch 'git push origin --force'` is not. The header comment's "is still denied, as the previous guard did" (about `watch 'sudo ls'` and `find -exec sh -c '...'`) holds for those four strings only. The unquoted `watch git push origin --force` is denied by the wrapper scan (the previous guard let it through). One form is misread by the lexer rather than invisible, and neither guard stops it, so it is not a regression: a backslash-newline between `$` and `(` inside double quotes, as in `git commit -m "$\<newline>(id)"` and `echo "$\<newline>(git push origin --force)"`, and the same two characters in the body of a heredoc with an unquoted delimiter fed to `git commit -F -`. `lex_dollar` looks at only the one character after `$` to find a `(` or `{`, so it takes the `$` as a lone character and the `(...)` after it as plain text, and the sentinel's four rules match neither `(id)` nor `git push origin --force` (the rule needs `git push --force` or `git push -f`). With `echo SUBST-RAN` as the stand-in command (at facd295b), bash 3.2.57 and dash ran the substitution in the double-quoted form, zsh 5.9 printed it as text, and all three ran it in the heredoc-body form. The same forms around a string the sentinel knows, `echo "$\<newline>(sudo ls)"` and `echo "$\<newline>(git push --force)"`, are denied, because the backslash-newline leaves the command with no data region. (c) Without awk (missing, or exiting non-zero) the four substring rules of the previous guard decide (the `awk_status` block after the awk program): `sudo ` anywhere, `git push --force` or `git push -f`, `git reset --hard`, and a `git commit` ... `-m ` message that starts with a double quote and holds `$(` or a backtick. With awk removed from `PATH` (62 probes) and with an awk that exits 2 (19 probes), jq present, every probe gave the previous guard's decision. They keep the old false positives (`echo 'never use sudo here'`, `visudo -c`, `my-sudo ls`, `git commit -m 'drop --force and git reset --hard from docs'`, and the recommended HEREDOC commit form are denied) and the old misses (`git push origin --force`, `git push origin -f`, `git -C x reset --hard`, and `sudo` followed by a tab pass). awk is a POSIX tool present on macOS and the usual Linux images, so this is a safety net more than a mode anyone runs. (d) ~~The guard file is 1596 lines at 899fff16, the last commit of fix/guard-msg-param-flag that changed it (1487 at e77e2937, the last commit of fix/guard-zsh-data-gaps that changed it, which is also the guard at c3a9242e; 1461 at 6b1f7acc, which is also the guard at 0931f791; 1458 at 12e9a9ad, 1442 at e5c9e6be), over the 800-line file-size guideline; the awk program alone is 1380 lines (from the `awk '` on line 174 to its closing quote on line 1553 at 899fff16; 1280 lines, from line 165 to line 1444, at e77e2937; 1260 lines, from line 159 to line 1418, at 6b1f7acc; lines 156 to 1415 at 12e9a9ad, 1246 lines at e5c9e6be).~~ Closed in refactor/guard-awk-split: da3b55d2 moved the awk program byte for byte (`cmp` against lines 175 to 1552 at 0abfede5) into `pre_bash_guard_lex.awk`, `pre_bash_guard_commands.awk`, and `pre_bash_guard_rules.awk`, which the script reads with `awk -f`; at 8c61c6cb, the last commit that changed them (measured at 1b02beef), the script is 231 lines and the three files 616, 336, and 465 lines (230, 618, 336, and 468 at c3a323aa). (e) ~~Status after refactor/guard-awk-split: every comment-only item below except the `SQ` spelling was fixed there (7347fa6a, f593bbfc, c3a323aa, and 8c61c6cb for the comment findings of the self-review; the single-quote rule went away with the split, since the shell no longer passes the program in single quotes), and the code items stay open with that one comment item: the loop that counts trailing backslashes, which `read_body` writes twice, and the reserved-word and `exec` rules of `end_cmd`, which decide nothing since the allowlist, with the guard header and the comment above `in_data` that still list them as separate reasons. The awk comments still spell a single quote as `SQ`, which the split no longer requires; no decision depends on it.~~ Closed in refactor/guard-debt-cleanup: b312dc80 spelled the quotes of the awk comments as real quotes, and d7dac506 merged the two trailing-backslash loops of `read_body` into `trailing_backslashes`, removed the reserved-word and `exec` rules of `end_cmd` with `RESW` and `EXEC_SEEN`, and moved the compound-command and exec cases of the header and `in_data` comments under the allowlist. No decision changed (1,912 runs against a0094fe5, 0 differences). The premise the two rules guarded is now a test: section F of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` checks that the DATACMD array holds no reserved word and no wrapper that `cmd_pos` steps past (the test runs the three `.awk` files with an extra `BEGIN` that prints DATACMD, so a name added in any way is read, not only one added to `datacmd_list`; the wrapper names are read from the `nm == "…"` comparisons in `cmd_pos`), and `exec >run.sh; echo 'sudo ls'` in `guard_deny_only_forms` turns red when `exec` is added to it. The record as it stood before: comments still open at 6b1f7acc (the first six come from self-review P2-6 and a note in the test report, the rest from self-review C3-3 to C3-5; each was checked again against 6b1f7acc in this sync, except the equivalent-mutant claim about the reserved-word and `exec` rules, which stands on the cycle 3 test report): `cmd_pos` sets `EXEC_SEEN` as a side effect that `end_cmd` reads after `judge`, and the comment on `cmd_pos` does not say so; the rule that no single quote may appear in the awk program is written only in the comment of `lex_dollar`, not at the start of the program; `read_body` writes the loop that counts trailing backslashes twice (for the first line and for each joined line); the comment before `new_ctx` lists the per-context arrays without `HPQ` and `HPN`; the header of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` (item B) does not list the kinds of form that `guard_deny_only_forms` added, and the paragraph before that array stops at group 8, while the later groups have their comments only inside the array: group 9 (the cross-review cycle 2 forms and the allowlist forms), a second comment that is also numbered 9 (the cross-review cycle 3 forms: printf with a `%`, `test -v`, `[ -v`), and two unnumbered ones (self-review C4-1, cycle 4 /test); the comment before group 1 of `guard_deny_only_forms` says two of its forms "were false none before", though `(echo sudo ls)` and the `if grep -q` form only print text and are denied now because a top-level group or compound command gets no data region (the previous guard denied them too); the reserved-word rule of `end_cmd` (a first word in `RESW`) and the `exec` rule (`EXEC_SEEN` with a redirection) decide nothing since the allowlist, because the first word of a command they catch is neither a data command nor `git`, so `data_first_ok` already drops the data regions, and `cmd_pos` does not step past prefixes when the first word is a data command or `git`, so `EXEC_SEEN` stays 0 there; removing either rule or both (mutations N03 and N04) changed no decision in the 507 probe rows of the test report, cycle 3, so they join J02, L03, and N02 as equivalent mutants, while the opening-parenthesis rule (`lex_cmds`) and the delimiter rule (`lex_redir`) each still decide alone; the guard header and the comment above `in_data` still list the two rules as separate reasons, and the `EXEC_SEEN` item above is moot until they go (self-review C3-3); the comment above the delimiter rule in `lex_redir` says the lexer "expands no `$"..."`, `${...}` or substitution", as if the shell did, but a heredoc delimiter of `${x}` or `$x` is read literally in bash, zsh, and dash (checked on 2026-10-08 with a body that holds `END` lines), and so is one in backticks except that dash reports a syntax error (self-review C3-4); only the escapes of `$'...'` beyond `\n`, `\t`, and `\r`, and `$"..."`, are read differently by the lexer and the shells, so the rule stops the form for a narrower reason than the comment gives, and the comment above `data_first_ok` calls its value what the shell sees, which fails for `$'...'` (the lexer decodes only `\n`, `\t`, and `\r`; `$'echo' 'sudo ls'` gets a data region, and the previous guard denies it); and three test comments in `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` (self-review C3-5): the `edge_deny` comment "Cycle 2 (P2-4)" and the `edge_none` comment "Cross-review cycle 2 (P2-4, P2-5)" use IDs that the self-review report gives to other findings (the triage numbers those two cross-review findings 4 and 5), and the `edge_sentinel_deny` comment "(change A, cycle 2)" names a change that no document defines (it is the allowlist, a9ef82b1); the comment above the allowlist forms in `guard_deny_only_forms` also lists "a variable as the command name" among the forms the allowlist closes, but `$c 'sudo ls'` is denied without it, and the comment that 8e94e76b added after that row says so, so that point is left. The comment drift of the cap-4 run (verify V5-1) was fixed in 6b1f7acc: the header now lists `pull` among the `--no-verify` subcommands, gives the abbreviation floor as one character after `--` (`--h` is `--hard`), and states the printf, rg, `test`, and `[` conditions in item 6(a), and the `no_verify_rules` comment and the comment before the abbreviation rows of the test file say the same (checked against 6b1f7acc). One piece of it is left: the header's "Not covered" paragraph still says the lexer misses the `$'…'` escapes and the sentinel sees only the text as written, without saying that printf and rg read their words as written (item 6(a) says it). Verify V5-3's other points are not guard debt: the plan's Scope lines for the `--no-verify` subcommands, the abbreviation floor, and the data commands keep the approved text because they are part of the approval digest, and the deviations are in the plan's Progress checklist. fix/guard-zsh-data-gaps (139d6627, e77e2937) left the items of (e) as they were: its diff of the guard changes the data-region text in the header, `lex_dollar`, `lex_hd`, `stage_note`, `msg_check`, and the `noexec_list` and `datacmd_list` lists with the comment of the second, and its diff of the test file changes the header's item B, group 10 of `guard_deny_only_forms`, `edge_deny`, and `edge_none` (checked by reading both diffs on 2026-10-09). The one change on the test side: the header's item B now names the zsh `${(e)…}` and `stat -A` kind, while group 10 has its comment only inside the array, so the paragraph before the array still stops at group 8. fix/guard-msg-param-flag (1e032dea, 43e73568) left the items of (e) as they were too (checked by reading its diff of the guard and of the test on 2026-10-09; the three comments of its own that self-review LOW 3 found were fixed in 899fff16), and group 11 of `guard_deny_only_forms` has its comment only inside the array as well. It adds two items. (1) The header comment's item 6(b), on the `-m` or `--message` value of git commit and git tag, lists the `$` that do not expand as one in single quotes or an ANSI-C string, an escaped one, and one followed by a blank, the end, or a closing double quote, but not `$"…"` outside double quotes, which `lex_dollar` treats as a `$` that does not expand: `git commit -m $"never sudo ls"` passes at 899fff16 and at c3a9242e, and the previous guard denies it (verify V-3; the behavior is unchanged by this PR, and the comment of `lex_dollar` is right). (2) Two test gaps from the test report. No row pins the zsh modifiers `=`, `^`, and `+` before a subscript: mutation M20, which drops `=` from `RE_NOTFLAG`, survives although it lets `echo $=arr['$(sudo ls)']` pass; that row in group 11 would pin it, and the previous guard denies it too. And the `$` that expands nothing are pinned only for the closing double quote (mutation M12): the mutations that drop the blank, tab, newline, or end-of-text condition (the M13 family) survive, and they err on the deny side, so a partial return of the over-denial that fix/guard-msg-param-flag closed would go unnoticed. The rows that would pin them, for `edge_none`, are in the test report: `git commit -m "costs $ 5; never sudo ls"` (a blank), the same with a tab or a newline after the `$`, and `rg 'sudo ' foo$` (the end). Both items were closed in refactor/guard-awk-split: 7347fa6a added `$"…"` to item 6(b) and the seven rows (three in group 11, four in `edge_none`; each flipped by its mutation), and f593bbfc added the row `git commit -m $"never sudo ls"` to `edge_none`. | (a) A harmless command is blocked and the agent has to rephrase it; a command written with `\` continuations, a read-only command inside a subshell, `if`, or `for`, behind a prefix or a path (`env echo`, `/bin/echo`), or in a call that also runs a command that is neither a data command nor `git`, and a recommended commit chained after `make test`, is blocked for that reason alone, even when the text is data; so are a `printf` with a `%`, `$`, or backtick in a word, a `test` or `[` command, and an `rg` with a `$'…'` or `$"…"` word (since fix/guard-msg-param-flag also one with a `$` that expands or a substitution; see (b) of its over-denials row below), none of which counts as a data command any more. The `--no-verify` rule also stops a merge message that is the text `--no-verify`, and abbreviations that git rejects anyway. (b) A deny rule can be evaded by indirection, by a string given to a runner, or by a backslash-newline between `$` and `(` (~~a variable word that rg reads as `--pre`~~, closed in fix/guard-msg-param-flag, commit 1e032dea), so the guard is a speed bump, not a sandbox; Claude Code's own permission checks are the other layer. (c) On a host without awk, the old false denies come back and `git push origin --force` is not stopped. No `ask` is involved in any case. (d) ~~The file is harder to read and change than the guideline intends.~~ Closed in refactor/guard-awk-split: the four files are 231, 616, 336, and 465 lines, each under the 800-line guideline. | (a) is the price of two design choices, both accepted in the plan's Design decisions: the wrapper scan exempts only commands known not to run their arguments, so `find -exec sudo`, `watch sudo ls`, and `flock /tmp/l git push --force` stay denied (the old guard denied them too) without a list of runners that would always miss one; and the sentinel exempts only the arguments of commands that read data, so text the lexer cannot follow stays denied as the previous guard denied it. The false positives of the no-data-region cases are the price of a third choice. The cross-review, a consult, and the cycle 2 /test (F2-1) showed forms in which a data region let through a command the shell runs: a force push or `sudo` in a group, a heredoc read across `$(...)`, a joined heredoc delimiter, `exec` with a redirection, and a backslash-newline between `$` and `(`. Those forms broke AC7 (a command the previous guard denied stays denied unless every match lies in a real data region), so the fix withholds the data region where the guard cannot tell where a read-only command's text ends up, instead of teaching the lexer each shape. It was made in the last pipeline run the default cap allows (`RALPH_STANDARD_MAX_PIPELINE_CYCLES`, 2), so it went in the stopping direction only. The cross-review of that run found three more forms of the same kind (a heredoc delimiter of `$'\x45'`, `env -S` with a split string, and `builtin exec >file`), and the cap was raised to 3. The allowlist (a9ef82b1) replaced the per-shape conditions with one rule, a data region only where every top-level command starts with a data command or `git`, again in the stopping direction only: it closed the `env -S` and `builtin exec` forms, and the delimiter rule closed the `$'\x45'` form (test report, cycle 3, Mutation). The cap-4 run went the same way. The cross-review of the cap-3 run (triage, cycle 3) found two more forms: zsh's `printf '%n'` and `test -v` (and `[ -v`) run a substitution in an argument that the guard had treated as data, and the `--no-verify` scan stopped at a `--` that is the value of `-m` or `--trailer`. The user raised the cap to 4 (cycle-count.json stays at 2) and b3c3fdaa fixed both inline. The self-review of that run found two more (C4-1: `$'\x25n'`, which zsh reads as `%n` and the lexer as `x25n`; C4-2: git accepts a one-letter option prefix, and `git reset --h` is a hard reset), which 12e9a9ad fixed by reading printf and rg words as written, lowering the `opt_is` floor to one character after `--`, and adding `pull`. Both fixes went in the stopping direction only: in the cycle 4 self-review and verify comparisons of the test examples with the previous guard, the rows that the previous guard denies and the new guard passes are still the 13 of `intentional_fixes`, and 12e9a9ad changed no row from deny to none; /test then killed 20 of 20 mutants of the new conditions (8 of them had survived until it added 7 rows). The user named that run the last one, so the items of (e) and the rg variable word of (b) stayed open (the rg variable word was closed in fix/guard-msg-param-flag, commit 1e032dea; the comment items of (e) were closed in refactor/guard-awk-split except the `SQ` spelling, and its code items stayed open until refactor/guard-debt-cleanup). The backslash-newline rule also stops forms where the two characters are only text; narrowing it to the forms the shell joins needs the `lex_dollar` fix first, which changes the paths from `lex_dq`, `lex_brace`, `lex_hd`, and `lex_word` (test report, cycle 2, F2-1, Proposed fix (1); self-review, cycle 2 re-run, Tech debt identified). Adding a command to `noexec_list` does not clear its false positive by itself: in a scratch copy of the guard, adding `apt-get`, `flock`, and `bash` to `noexec_list` left `apt-get remove sudo -y`, `apt-get install sudo vim`, `bash -c 'x' sudo ls`, and `flock l git grep sudo file` denied, and adding `apt-get` to `datacmd_list` as well cleared the two `apt-get` forms. `datacmd_list` is for commands that only read data, so putting `apt-get` there is a design call. (b) The first group needs run-time information that a hook payload does not carry (the plan's Non-goals). The runner-string gap is not a regression, since the previous guard let the same strings through, and closing it means a list of string-taking runners and their options, the kind of list the plan's Design decisions chose not to depend on. The `$`, backslash-newline, `(` gap waits for the `lex_dollar` change above; the previous guard misses it too. (c) keeps the old guard's behavior where the new one cannot run. (d) ~~Moving the awk program into its own file adds a second core file under `.claude/hooks/` that the guard must find at run time and that existing installs must receive; whether that needs code changes in the manifest or in `internal/cli/migrate.go` (it names the guard at line 77) was not checked, and the plan scoped the work to one script.~~ Closed in refactor/guard-awk-split: the split needed no change to the manifest or to `internal/cli/migrate.go`. With ralph built from 6dab8a5d, `ralph init` and `ralph upgrade` (on a tree that the base ralph had initialized) deliver the three `.awk` files as core files (upgrade: created 3, updated 2), and `legacyRalphHookCommands` in `internal/cli/migrate.go` lists only the hook commands that settings.json calls, which the `.awk` files are not. The one-time migration from a v1 layout, which chains into the same upgrade engine, was not run (verify report, Coverage gaps). (e) Fixing a comment changes the guard, and a changed guard starts another post-implementation pipeline run (`post-implementation-pipeline.md`); the cycle cap was already spent, and spent again in the cap-3 run and the cap-4 run (self-review, cycle 2, 3, and 4, Tech debt identified; the cycle 4 list there is not carried over, because 12e9a9ad fixed its C4-1 to C4-4 and 6b1f7acc the comment drift that verify V5-1 found). refactor/guard-awk-split fixed the comment-only items in a change that keeps every decision, except the `SQ` spelling, which no decision depends on and which is not worth a change of its own (it touches comment lines in all three files); its code items stayed, because removing a rule or merging the loop changes code and needs mutation evidence of its own. refactor/guard-debt-cleanup settled them with that evidence. | (a) A report of a harmless command blocked by the wrapper scan or the sentinel: for the wrapper scan, add the command to `noexec_list` or teach `cmd_pos` the wrapper's options; for the sentinel (which also denies the wrapper-scan cases), add it to `datacmd_list` too or add a data region (`stage_note`, `pipe_decide`); then move its case from `edge_sentinel_deny` to `edge_none` in `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh`. For a command or commit message denied only because of a backslash-newline: first make `lex_dollar` skip a backslash-newline right after `$`, then narrow the backslash-newline rule to the forms the shell joins, and move its text-only cases in `edge_sentinel_deny` to `edge_none`. For a read-only command denied only by the allowlist (a prefix, a path, or another command in the same call): the first-word rule is what closed `env -S` and `builtin exec`, so widening `data_first_ok` is a design call, and the forms of section B of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` are its regression guard; a recommended commit chained after another command needs `git commit` as a command of its own, or `git commit -F <file>`. For a `printf`, `rg`, `test`, or `[` command denied only by the cap-4 conditions: each condition closes an execution path that was checked in zsh or bash (`%n`, `printf -v`, `test -v`, `rg --pre`), so loosening one is a design call; the regression guard is the printf, rg, `test -v`, and `[ -v` rows of section B and the six rows of `edge_sentinel_deny` that name them. For a harmless command denied by the `--no-verify` rule: only a message that is the text `--no-verify` and abbreviations git rejects are known, and narrowing the rule means reading the value-taking options of merge, rebase, am, and pull the way `commit_rules` does. (b) A report of a deny rule evaded through one of these forms; fix it only when the form can be read from the command text. ~~For the rg variable word, the next change to `stage_note`: leave rg out of the data commands for any word that holds a `$` (the choice self-review C4-1 recommended; the reports give no measurement of it for rg), and add `rg $x sudo pat .` to `edge_sentinel_deny` in the same commit.~~ Done in fix/guard-msg-param-flag: rg is left out of the data commands for a word with a `$` that expands, ANSI-C or locale quoting, or a substitution, and `rg $x sudo pat .` is pinned in group 11 of `guard_deny_only_forms` (section B of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh`). (e) ~~The next change to `read_body` or to the reserved-word and `exec` rules of `end_cmd`: settle the code items of (e) and the comments that name them in the same change; the `SQ` spelling of a single quote goes with the next change that edits an awk comment where it appears (the other comment-only items were fixed in refactor/guard-awk-split, after this trigger fired at fix/guard-zsh-data-gaps and fix/guard-msg-param-flag, which carried them over).~~ Done in refactor/guard-debt-cleanup; <!-- RESOLVED 2026-10-07 in fix/guard-deny-only (Slice 2, commit e48797a2): the substring matching that took the time was replaced by an awk lexer that reads the command through a 512-character window (BWK awk, the macOS awk, copies the whole string on every `substr()` call, so a character-by-character read would have been quadratic). The 28 s payload (a 200,000-character quoted string, then `git commit -m` with a command substitution) is pinned as `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` section H, which fails above 10 s on the jq-absent path; the Slice 2 measurement was 0.28 to 0.83 s on macOS awk, and a re-run on 2026-10-07 of a 208,526-byte `echo "<long quoted string>" && git commit -m x` finished in under 1 s with and without jq. Row preserved for traceability. -->
| ~~`pre_bash_guard.sh` is slow on very long commands: a 200 KB quoted string followed by `git commit -m` took 28 s. Commands up to 100 KB stay under 0.15 s on both paths.~~ (RESOLVED 2026-10-07 in fix/guard-deny-only — the matching was replaced; a 200 KB command takes under 1 s) | A Bash call stalls until the hook times out, and a hook that times out decides nothing. Only a command that carries a very large literal reaches it, such as a heredoc that writes a big file. | Existing behavior. | Test gaps around the Bash guard and two neighboring tests (carried over from guard-bypass-and-hygiene and rewritten for fix/guard-deny-only, then updated after S2c, the tests that /test added in 39ed2759, and pipeline cycles 2 to 4, and again after fix/guard-zsh-data-gaps; that PR's test report lists them as Test gaps 1 to 9; refactor/guard-awk-split added (c) and (g) from its own test report; refactor/guard-debt-cleanup added (h) and (i) from its self-review and test report). Closed since: the write-target forms (`cat >.env<<EOF`, `/usr/bin/tee`, the accidental `tee` forms) and the untested `plan`, `acceptEdits`, and `dontAsk` modes went with the ask rules (the guard reads no `permission_mode`; section A of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh` runs it with the key absent, `default`, `auto`, and `bypassPermissions`); whether a live payload carries `permission_mode: "bypassPermissions"` no longer matters, since nothing reads it; and the first real `archive-plan.sh` rewrite happened in 9b48eb86 (it moved the plan and rewrote the three path references to it in this file). Open: (a) The limits in the guard-limits row above are partly pinned (section D of `tests/test-pre-bash-guard.sh`). Pinned since 39ed2759: the three wrapper-scan false positives (`apt-get remove sudo -y`, `bash -c 'x' sudo ls`, `flock l git grep sudo file`) as deny in `edge_sentinel_deny`, with a comment to move each to `edge_none` once fixed; the sentinel's inherited false positives `cp sudo dest`, `apt-get install sudo 2>/dev/null`, `git push --force-if-includes origin main`, and `grep -r 'sudo ' . \| sort` as deny; `apt-get install sudo` as none (section C) and `bash script.sh` as none; every place whose text is re-read as commands, each with a form that only the lexer denies (the block of `edge_deny` that starts "Forms only the lexer denies"); and the edges of the guard's 512-character text window and index block (`check_named`: a match across the window edge is denied, a data region longer than one block is still data). Not pinned: the blind spots (`git commit -m "$msg"`, `$cmd`, `cat file \| sh`, `git config core.hooksPath /dev/null`, `echo "$(id)" \| git commit -F -`, `ssh host 'git push origin --force'`, and the static expansions `{su,}do ls`, `?udo ls`, `[s]udo ls`, `$'\x73udo' ls`); the runner-string gap (`watch 'git push origin --force'`, `find . -exec sh -c 'git push origin --force' \;`); and two false positives that no case names, `apt-get install sudo vim` and the `\| sed` form of a read-only pipeline (`\| sort` is pinned). Also not pinned: the lexer-only gap of the guard-limits row, (b), where `$`, a backslash-newline, and `(` inside double quotes are not stopped by either guard (no test has an example). Both of its forms are still none at e5c9e6be, at facd295b, and in the previous guard (probed on 2026-10-08), so a test could only pin the gap as none. Pinned since 46806dc9 and 4e829e34: the no-data-region cases (`guard_deny_only_forms` in section B, the text-only backslash-newline forms in `edge_sentinel_deny`, and the joined-heredoc forms that only the lexer denies in `edge_deny`). Three mutations of those mechanisms cannot be pinned because they are equivalent mutants, which the tester found (test report, pipeline cycle 2 re-run, Mutation): J02 (the joined-body rule in `read_body` ignored), L03 (`HBSNL` ignored), and N02 (`NODATA` not set at an unmatched `)`) leave all 1730 tests green (the suite at facd295b). After 4e829e34 a body or delimiter with a backslash-newline already drops every data region, and an unmatched `)` occurs only in a `case` pattern, where `case` and `esac` set `NODATA`. The other 19 of the 22 mutations turn at least one test red; the 22 were chosen by hand and no coverage was measured. Pinned since a9ef82b1, 849f5411, and the cycle 3 /test (8e94e76b): the allowlist (forms in `guard_deny_only_forms` with a prefix, a path, a variable, or a redirect-only command in front of a data command, and `"echo" 'sudo ls'` in `edge_none` for the quote-stripped first word), the delimiter rule for a `$` (the `$'\x45'` form), the value-taking options of `git push` (`-o`, `--push-option`, `--repo`, with their abbreviated and bundled forms, in `edge_none` and `edge_deny`), and the `--` stop and the `--pathspec-from-file` value of `git reset` (`edge_none` and `edge_deny`). The cycle 3 /test ran 27 mutations of those rules; 11 of them stayed green until it added 11 rows, and now 24 turn at least one test red and 3 survive. AL02 (the allowlist's check for a `/` in the first word) is an equivalent mutant, since `git` and the `DATACMD` names never hold a `/`. DL02 (the backtick half of the delimiter rule) and DL05 (a top-level condition on that rule) are defensive: none of the shells checked (bash, zsh, dash) reads a backtick delimiter differently from the lexer, and no test has a heredoc inside a substitution whose delimiter holds a `$` or a backtick. The reserved-word and `exec` rules (N03, N04) became equivalent when the allowlist went in, like N02. refactor/guard-debt-cleanup (d7dac506) removed those two rules, so N03 and N04 no longer exist, and the equivalent mutants are now J02, L03, N02, and AL02. Pinned since b3c3fdaa, 12e9a9ad, and the cycle 4 /test (389436de): the printf conditions (a `%`, a `$`, a backtick, and `-v`, including `printf $'\x25n' …` and `printf $'\x2dv' …`; `guard_deny_only_forms` in section B and `edge_sentinel_deny`), the rg conditions (`--pre`, `$'…'`, and `$"…"`), `test -v` and `[ -v` (section B) with the `test`, `[`, and `%` false positives (`edge_sentinel_deny`), the `--no-verify` scan across `--` for merge and rebase and the value-taking long options of `git commit`, also abbreviated and with an `=` value (`edge_deny` and `edge_none`), and the one-letter prefixes and `pull` (`edge_deny`). The cycle 4 /test ran 20 mutations of those conditions (printf 6, rg 4, `DATACMD` 3, `no_verify_rules` 1, `commit_rules` 3, `opt_is` 2, `pull` 1); 8 of them stayed green until it added 7 rows, and now all 20 turn at least one test red, with no equivalent mutant among them; the 20 were chosen by hand and no coverage was measured. (b) to (d) stay: they are denied by the previous guard too, and no report has come in. | `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-09-guard-zsh-data-gaps.md` (Risks, Progress checklist; `/pr` rewrites this path when it archives the plan); `docs/reports/verify-2026-10-09-guard-zsh-data-gaps.md` (V-4) |
| Over-denials that fix/guard-msg-param-flag (commits 1e032dea and 43e73568) adds to `pre_bash_guard.sh`, each also denied by the previous guard (so AC7 of guard-deny-only holds), checked by the S1 implementer on 2026-10-09 with and without jq. (a) A commit or tag message word with a `$` that is not followed by a blank, the end, or a closing double quote is not data: a variable, `$1`, `$@`, `${…}`, and also some `$` that bash leaves as text, such as `5$,`. Such a message that also holds a sentinel word is denied: `git commit -m "use $1 never sudo ls"` and `git commit -m "costs 5$, never sudo ls"` (none at c3a9242e, deny now and in the previous guard; probed on 2026-10-09 with and without jq). A message without a sentinel word, such as `git commit -m "${msg}"` or `git commit -m "$msg"`, still passes. (b) rg with a word that has an expanding `$`, ANSI-C or locale quoting, or a substitution is not a data command, so `rg $1 'sudo ' .` and `rg "$@" 'sudo ' .` are denied. (c) From a `$` that starts a zsh subscript (`$name[`, `$~name[`, `$[`, and the special parameters and lengths such as `$@[` and `$#x[`) to the end of its word, nothing is data, so a sentinel word after the subscript in the same word is denied (`echo $a[$b[1]]'sudo ls'`). In bash the `[…]` after `$a` is only text, so this denies more than bash needs. | A harmless commit message, rg call, or argument that mentions a sentinel word next to an expanding `$` is blocked and has to be rephrased (for a commit message, `git commit -F <file>` avoids it). | Telling which expansions zsh or bash evaluate as code from those that only substitute a value would need the variable values, which a hook does not see; the mark errs on the deny side, as the plan's Design decisions state. | A report of a blocked command of one of these shapes. | `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-09-guard-msg-param-flag.md` (Design decisions, Progress checklist; `/pr` rewrites this path when it archives the plan) |
| `LegacyWorktreeStateDir` (`internal/org/statedir.go`, the `os.Stat` at about `:100` and the `samePath(candidate, resolvedDir)` check after it) stats `<show-toplevel>/.harness/state/org/manifest.jsonl` before it checks that this candidate is not the resolved state dir itself. In the main checkout the two are the same path, so when the main checkout's own ledger directory cannot be read (permission denied, or `.harness/state` is a file) the stat error is reported as a linked worktree's older ledger: `ralph status` and `ralph insights` print `note: cannot read this linked worktree's older org ledger (...)`, and the mutating verbs refuse with `cannot tell whether this linked worktree's older ledger still has active seats` (verify V-1, probe P4). | `ralph org stop --all` and `disband --all` have two robustness gaps (self-review F-3 and F-4 of org-stop-all). (a) F-3, no overall time bound: each herdr or agmsg call has its own 10 s deadline (`defaultDriverCallTimeout` in `internal/org/verbs.go`), and when herdr accepts a connection but never answers, a seat fails at its first ownership `get` after about 10 s (`stopSeatPane`). `StopAll` (`internal/org/verbs_all.go`) therefore takes about 10 s per active seat (about 5 minutes for 30) and prints its result only at the end (`printStopAllResult` in `internal/cli/org.go`). A refused connection fails at once, so only a herdr that never answers is slow. (b) F-4, `Disband` reads the manifest once (its first `Manifest.Read`), stops the seats it saw, and writes `disbanded` last (`appendDisbanded`). A seat that a concurrent `spawn` adds after that read is not stopped, yet is inactive once `disbanded` is written. This PR widened the window by three `get` calls per seat. | (a) Against a hung herdr the operator sees no output for minutes. Nothing is lost: each seat that was not stopped stays active (`stop_failed`) and is listed at the end. (b) A seat started while its org is being disbanded can keep running although the roster no longer lists it as active. It needs a `spawn` and a `disband` running at the same time. | Both are LOW findings the self-review sent to this ledger, and neither blocks the merge. (b) Concurrent spawn and disband becoming a normal pattern (per-feature orgs): re-read the manifest just before `appendDisbanded` and return a failure when an active seat that the first read did not have has appeared. | `docs/plans/archive/2026-10-07-org-stop-all.md`; `docs/reports/self-review-2026-10-07-org-stop-all.md` (appendix, cycle 1: F-3, F-4) |

| Wording findings, an unused function, and test gaps of the org-limits-reserve cycle 4 fix, the reservation rule that `reserveAgain` and `releasedReservation` apply on both the pane path and the workspace path (cycle 4: self-review C4-1 to C4-4, verify V4-1, test T4-6 to T4-8; all LOW). After `disband --force` fails to close the caller's own pane (the seat is `stopped`, the org is `disbanded`, the process is alive, and `--force` compensates nothing), a `stop` from that pane appends `stopped` after `disbanded`; if that close fails again, `reactivateSeat` makes the seat active and `reserveAgain` finds no start after `disbanded` (`stopped` is not one of the starts that `startsOrg` counts), so it restores the reservation that the forced disband released. A `disband` whose own compensation could not be written (the lock, the second read, or the append failed) leaves the same input for a retried `stop` (verify V4-1, read from the code, not run). The user decided on 2026-10-09 to keep restoring it: the seat is treated as running again, and the reservation comes back with it, as it does after a `disband` run again. `TestOrgCloseDeferredSelfPane_StopRetriedAfterForcedDisband_ReservationRestored` pins this, and the provisional fix that skips `reserveAgain` when the seat's `stopped` follows `disbanded` (cycle 4 Q4) turns it red. The window is as long as the operator takes between the forced disband and the retried `stop`, not the tens of seconds that the plan's Risks give. (b) C4-2: the term `ActiveReservation(now, orgID) != nil` of `reserveAgain` is documented as an independent condition ("and gives the org no reservation"), but the scan of `now[d+1:]` includes it: when `now[d] == before[d]`, the latest `disbanded` of `now` is at d or later, so the `scope_reserved` that makes the reservation non-nil lies in `now[d+1:]`, and `startsOrg` counts `scope_reserved`. Cycle 4 mutation K1, which drops the term, is equivalent. (c) C4-3: `reservationBeforeLastDisband` (`internal/org/reserve.go`) has no production caller. Only `TestReservationBeforeLastDisband` calls it, and its `held none in its last life` case (`[reservation, disbanded, disbanded]` gives nil) pins the opposite of what `releasedReservation` returns for the same input. A later change that picks the old function would bring back the defect that 83aec44e fixed (no reservation restored after a `disband` run again); its 100% function coverage comes from that test alone (test T4-6). (d) C4-4: four leftovers of 83aec44e. The doc of `CloseDeferredSelfWorkspace` has one line left unwrapped after the insert (41 columns among lines of the seventies). In the doc of `CloseDeferredSelfPane` the reservation sentences sit between "the error says the seat is recorded active again" and "It decides that from the manifest read again under the manifest lock", so "that" now points at the whole compensation, not the seat. `compensateUnderLock` hands `compensate` one snapshot of the ledger, read before any of its appends, and `reserveAgain` depends on it: the `spawned` that `reactivateSeat` appends counts as a start in `startsOrg`, so a second read between the steps would make `reserveAgain` skip, taking the seat's own return for a restart. No doc says that this call's appends are not in `now`, and the doc of `reserveAgain` calls `now` "the manifest read under the lock" without saying it is one snapshot (three tests pin it indirectly; cycle 4 mutations K19 and K20 turn them red). The expression "the org was started after d" (`slices.ContainsFunc(..., startsOrg)`) appears in both `releasedReservation` and `reserveAgain`, so the two steps stay one rule only while the copies agree. (e) Test gaps: T4-7 (verify V4-1, second input; a probe, not committed): an org that a new ralph started with `--reserve`, an older ralph disbanded without closing its workspace, and a new ralph then disbands again from inside that workspace with a failing close, gets back the reservation that the older ralph's `disbanded` released (the two `disbanded` records follow each other, and `releasedReservation` reads the reservation at the start before them, where `reservationBeforeLastDisband` would have returned nil); it needs a ledger shared by an old and a new binary. (a) L5(a): `StartFeatureResult.Split` has no reader in production code, only tests (`readStartFeature` returns the plan on an error for them). (b) L5(b): `ResolveSplitPlanPath` returns the plan id, `readStartFeature` discards it, and `LoadSplitPlan` derives the same id from the path again. (c) L5(c): `splitFeatureDraft.dependsLine` copies a value that `fieldLine[splitFieldDependsOn]` already holds. (d) L5(d): the last line of the `HerdrClient` comment in `internal/org/spawn.go` is 91 columns wide where the others are 78 or fewer. (e) L5(e): `EnsureWorktree` is the request struct of `FeatureWorktrees.Ensure` but has a verb's name, so `Ensure(repoRoot, EnsureWorktree{...})` reads as an action. (f) N1: `TestRenderRolePrompt_Leader_FeatureOrgProcedure` pins two phrases that contain a line break of `leader.md` (the one that starts "手順 1 の" and the `--id` sentence), so re-wrapping the template without changing its meaning fails the test; the older pins in the same file read a whole list item with `markdownItem`. (g) N2: the opening of "機能ごとの org" in `leader.md` allows writes outside the reservation only for the leader's own steps 1, 4, and 5, but the reviewer also writes its report under `docs/reports/` (live run); the seat's own role prompt covers it, so the effect is small. (h) V-3: `checkHerdrAvailable` and `checkAgmsgAvailable` (`internal/cli/doctor.go`) end their `info` detail with "(solo execution unaffected)", wording from the Solo formation that this branch retired; the skill's matching sentence now says the standard flow without an org. (i) Found by sync-docs: the number 20 (`maxFeatureOrgIDLen`) is written in the `ralph org start --help` text, in the `--org-id` usage, and in three places of the `/org` skill (four copies), and no test ties those texts to the constant. (j) C2-3: N1 came back in a third form: `TestRenderRolePrompt_Leader_FeatureOrgProcedure` looks for "の行にある`--state-dir` をそのまま付けて" after removing every newline from the intro of "機能ごとの org", so it passes only while that intro breaks its line between "にある" and "`--state-dir`" (a break at a space, as between "`- 台帳:`" and "の行", would fail it); the sync-docs of the second pipeline run edited the sentences after those two lines and left the lines alone for this reason. (k) C2-4: the comment of `parseSplitPlan` in `split.go` ("A line of a fenced code block is never a heading or a field line: in a feature it is body, elsewhere it is read as any other text there is") and the file's header comment ("no line in it is a heading or a field line") say more than the code does: a `- Status:` or `- Approved:` line inside a fence in the header still counts as a header line (a second one is refused), and after the header the lines the digest does not read are refused even inside a fence; the code and `TestLoadSplitPlan_Rejects` are right. (l) C2-5: `shellQuote` in `feature.go` is a second copy of the quoting branch of `shellQuoteIfNeeded` in `internal/cli/org.go` (`cli` imports `org`, so one exported `org.ShellQuote` could serve both); and the `mustAbs` call around `p.StateDir` in `StartFeature` does nothing for the absolute, resolved `StateDir` that `runOrgStartPlan` passes, has no comment saying why it is there, and would write the cwd as the ledger if `StateDir` were empty (no caller passes it empty) | (a) to (e) cost a reader a moment. | Test gaps left by org-feature-worktree (the test report's T-1 to T-7, and T2-1 to T2-6 of its cycle 2). (a) T-1: nothing runs a real herdr with a 20-character org_id and the seat `implementer`; the 32-character agent-name limit is checked on ralph's side (`checkSpawnInput`, fake herdr) and the live run used the slug `hello` (5 characters). (b) T-2: of the failures of `ralph-worktree.sh ensure`, a state collision cannot be reached through `start` with the real script (the reuse check reads the record first and refuses before `ensure`; only a record that changes in between gets there), and a missing `jq`, a missing default branch, and the `.codex/config.toml` rewrite run only against a fake `ensure` plus `TestEnsureFailureMessages_InWorktreeScript`, which checks that the script still contains the six matched strings. (c) T-3: a split plan whose line endings change from LF to CRLF after its approval changes the digest (on a probe, `8fdeb8b031c9` for LF and `7b450efec517` for CRLF), so `start` should refuse it as changed after its approval, as `scripts/plan-visual.sh digest` would; no test pins that (the CRLF tests approve a CRLF plan). (d) T-4: concurrent starts, see the concurrent-start row above. (e) T-5: the two error branches of `runOrgStartPlan` (`newOrgSpawnRuntimeAt` and `resolveModelOrWarn` failing) are not run; the model refusal itself is covered one layer down by the "a model outside the pool" case of `TestStartFeature_RefusedBeforeAnyRecord`. (f) T-6: the defensive branches of `StartFeature` (the leader's input check and a failing ledger read) and of `scriptFeatureWorktrees` (empty or non-JSON output, a failing `git branch --show-current`) are not run. (g) T-7: the live run (AC14) used af138af6; ~~nothing re-ran it after 8aae7ce2 and 2c1bb13b (the 20-character check, the `implementer` and `reviewer` id sentence in `leader.md`)~~ (Run 4 of the evidence file re-ran it on a binary built at 9a2dc5ef, after those and after d49bbc34, 07d38e6d, and 9c1d447f, with a non-default ledger: the leader followed the `- 台帳:` line, every `ralph org` command carried `--state-dir`, no default ledger appeared, and the seats had the ids `implementer` and `reviewer`; that closes test T2-1 and self-review C2-6 (f); the 20-character boundary with a real herdr stays in (a)), and the cases where `git push` and `gh pr create` succeed (a repo with a remote) and where the leader is a codex seat were never run. (h) T2-2: nobody has looked at what environment the leader's herdr pane gets; ralph passes it no state dir (no code sets `--state-dir` or `RALPH_ORG_STATE_DIR` for a pane), and the skill, the leader template, and the `featureLeaderTask` comment now say only that the pane's environment is not necessarily the one start ran in; a live check (start with `RALPH_ORG_STATE_DIR` set and a leader that prints its environment) would settle whether it reaches the pane. (i) T2-3: `ralph org start <task>` without `--plan` and with a non-default ledger gives the leader no ledger; the version-skew row above holds the gap and its options. (j) T2-4: the symlink case of `TestLedgerMainWorktreeRoot` needs an existing ledger; for a ledger that does not exist yet and is named through a symlinked alias (macOS `/tmp/x` against the `/private/tmp/x` that git reports), `samePath` falls back to comparing strings, so that first `spawn` reads the limits of the cwd's `ralph.toml` and the next ones, once the ledger exists, those of the main worktree's; a feature org does not meet it because `start` creates the ledger before the leader's first command. (k) T2-5: for a split plan with an unclosed fence and, after it, a line the digest does not read (such as `## Progress checklist`), the digest-skipped line is refused first and the fence error comes after that fix; no test fixes which error comes first. (l) T2-6: the three probes of the cycle 2 test report (a `featureLeaderTask` line through `sh` to the real binary, `StartFeature` through to the prompt, `LoadSplitPlan` against the script's digest) were typed by hand and not kept; existing tests cover their parts | (a) a herdr that counts the name differently, or a changed herdr limit, ships unnoticed. (b) a reworded script message silently drops the matching hint to the script's plain text, since only the strings' presence is checked. (c) a regression in how a CRLF plan is read after approval ships unnoticed. (e), (f) a failure in an unrun branch is first seen by an operator. (g) the first real PR from a feature org may meet a failure that the live run could not show. (h) the docs could be wrong about the pane's environment in either direction; the leader's own `--state-dir` makes both cases work. (j) the first `spawn` under a symlinked, not-yet-existing ledger reads the limits of the cwd's `ralph.toml`, once.
